//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
use super::*;
use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};

pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
}

/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    loop {
        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
        let page_mark = meter.mark();
        let mut limit = PAGE;
        let mut page = loop {
            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
                    bytes = next;
                    count += 1;
                }
            }
            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
            let query = if after.is_some() {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            } else {
                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
            };
            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
            {
                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
            }
            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
            drop(page);
            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
        };
        for (id, data) in &page {
            let row_mark = meter.mark();
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
        }
        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
        drop(page);
        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn fixture(count: usize, payload: usize) -> Store {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        for n in 0..count {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(payload))),
            ]));
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        tx.commit().unwrap();
        store
    }

    #[test]
    fn callback_keeps_the_whole_fetched_page_reserved() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let mut serialized_bytes = 0;
        for n in 0..20 {
            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(1024))),
            ]));
            serialized_bytes += (id.len() + data.len()) as u64;
            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
        }
        let mut calls = 0;
        let mut first_heap = None;
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |_, row, meter| {
            let mut decoder = Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort);
            adoption::charge(&mut decoder, adoption::decode_bytes(&canonical_json(&row)))?;
            let decode = decoder.used.heap_bytes;
            // Independent lower bound: Rust-owned images and both native
            // payload copies coexist with decode. This excludes headers/slots,
            // so it does not just repeat the walker's allocation formula.
            assert!(meter.used.heap_bytes >= 3 * serialized_bytes + decode,
                "Rust and native page copies must coexist with decoder scratch: {:?}", meter.used);
            let expected = first_heap.get_or_insert(meter.used.heap_bytes);
            assert_eq!(meter.used.heap_bytes, *expected, "earlier rows release only their own scratch");
            assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, 20 + calls + 1, "all admission decodes plus callback decodes are charged");
            calls += 1;
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(calls, 20);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn heap_retries_keep_admission_examinations_at_the_callback_boundary() {
        let mut store = fixture(5, 1280 << 10);
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let work = adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 1, index_entries: 1 };
        let scratch = |row: &Value| adoption::contribution_bytes(row, &crate::schema::public_table(Default::default(), Default::default()));
        let mut observed = Vec::new();
        walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, Some(&scratch), |_, row, meter| {
            observed.push(meter.used.examined);
            assert_eq!((meter.used.steps, meter.used.index_entries), (0, 0), "rejected attempts perform no contribution work");
            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            meter.step(1)?;
            adoption::charge(meter, scratch(&row))?;
            meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, 1)?;
            assert!(meter.heap_peak <= Ceilings::job().heap_bytes);
            Ok(())
        }).unwrap();
        // Four two-image attempts each decode one row before the heap check
        // asks for a singleton. Its admission and callback decode add two.
        // The last source is already a singleton and needs no rejected attempt.
        assert_eq!(observed, [3, 3, 3, 3, 2]);
        tx.rollback().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_cumulative_budget_fixes_the_page_before_any_callback() {
        for (work, sizes) in [
            (adoption::Work { examined: 64, steps: 63, index_entries: 63 }, vec![252, 4]),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 5040, index_entries: 1 }, vec![198, 58]),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 0, index_entries: 400 }, vec![40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 16]),
        ] {
            let mut store = fixture(256, 0);
            let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
            let (mut page, mut offset, mut calls) = (0, 0u64, 0);
            walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, None, |_, _, meter| {
                assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, sizes[page] + offset * work.examined + 1,
                    "the entire admission pass and every earlier callback remain charged");
                assert_eq!(meter.used.steps, offset * work.steps);
                assert_eq!(meter.used.index_entries, offset * work.index_entries);
                meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, work.examined - 1)?;
                meter.step(work.steps)?;
                meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, work.index_entries)?;
                calls += 1;
                offset += 1;
                if offset == sizes[page] { page += 1; offset = 0; }
                Ok(())
            }).unwrap();
            assert_eq!(calls, 256);
            assert_eq!(page, sizes.len());
            tx.rollback().unwrap();
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn only_non_fitting_singletons_refuse_before_callbacks() {
        for (work, kind) in [
            (adoption::Work { examined: 16_384, steps: 0, index_entries: 0 }, MeterKind::Examined),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 1_000_001, index_entries: 0 }, MeterKind::Steps),
            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 0, index_entries: 16_385 }, MeterKind::IndexEntries),
        ] {
            for count in [0, 1] {
                let mut store = fixture(count, 0);
                let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
                let mut calls = 0;
                let result = walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, None, |_, _, _| { calls += 1; Ok(()) });
                assert_eq!(calls, 0, "no callback can start with an impossible page budget");
                if count == 0 { result.unwrap(); }
                else { assert!(matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: got, .. }) if got == kind), "{result:?}"); }
                tx.rollback().unwrap();
            }
        }
    }
}
//! A bounded serialized read-ahead buffer used only inside server activation.
//! Logical callers still fetch and price exactly one row at a time.
// @ref LLP 3000.002#implementation-follow-up — separate bounded raw workspace preserves logical admission.
use super::*;
use crate::{
    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
    rows::word_heap as heap,
};
use std::{cell::RefCell, collections::VecDeque};

pub(super) type Buffer = RefCell<Option<Page>>;

pub(super) struct Page {
    table: i64,
    index: i64,
    lo: Vec<u8>,
    hi: Vec<u8>,
    rows: VecDeque<(String, Vec<u8>)>,
    /// Retain the admission for the complete buffer, including consumed slots.
    _meter: Meter,
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{engine::InternalLedger, rows::Rows, value::object};

    fn fixture() -> (Store, Schema) {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table entries:\n  n: int\n  text: text <=200000\n  public\n  online only\nquery entries():\n  return entries first 20 by .n\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let schema: Schema =
            serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&bound.schema).unwrap()).unwrap();
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        store.install(&schema).unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        for n in 0..96 {
            let row = object([
                ("id", Value::Id(format!("r{n:03}"))),
                ("n", Value::Int(n)),
                (
                    "text",
                    Value::String("x".repeat(if n == 40 { 150_000 } else { 100 })),
                ),
            ]);
            tx.put("entries", &schema.tables["entries"], &row, None)
                .unwrap();
        }
        tx.commit().unwrap();
        (store, schema)
    }

    #[test]
    fn activation_read_ahead_preserves_rows_cursors_and_every_logical_charge() {
        let (mut store, schema) = fixture();
        let index = schema.tables["entries"]
            .indexes
            .iter()
            .find(|(_, i)| i.columns().first().is_some_and(|c| c == "n"))
            .unwrap()
            .0;
        let mut tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
        for n in 0..=96 {
            tx.activation_scan = None;
            let mut slow = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
            let a = InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut slow, |meter, scope| {
                Rows::scan_metered(
                    &tx,
                    "entries",
                    index,
                    &[],
                    &bounds,
                    Dir::Asc,
                    1,
                    scope,
                    meter,
                )
            })
            .unwrap();
            tx.activation_scan = Some(Default::default());
            // Fill/reuse the page by reading the predecessor first, where there is one.
            if n > 0 {
                let before = Bounds {
                    after: Some(vec![Value::Int(n - 2), Value::Id(format!("r{:03}", n - 2))]),
                    ..Default::default()
                };
                let mut ignored = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
                InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut ignored, |meter, scope| {
                    Rows::scan_metered(
                        &tx,
                        "entries",
                        index,
                        &[],
                        &before,
                        Dir::Asc,
                        1,
                        scope,
                        meter,
                    )
                })
                .unwrap();
            }
            let mut fast = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
            let b = InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut fast, |meter, scope| {
                Rows::scan_metered(
                    &tx,
                    "entries",
                    index,
                    &[],
                    &bounds,
                    Dir::Asc,
                    1,
                    scope,
                    meter,
                )
            })
            .unwrap();
            assert_eq!(a, b, "row {n}");
            assert_eq!(slow.used, fast.used, "meter {n}");
            if let Some((_, key)) = a.last() {
                bounds.after = crate::key::decode(key);
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn activation_read_ahead_is_invalidated_by_writes_and_raw_access() {
        let (mut store, schema) = fixture();
        let mut tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        tx.activation_scan = Some(Default::default());
        let t = tx.num("entries").unwrap();
        let ti = tx.num("entries.byId").unwrap();
        assert!(
            tx.activation_head(t, ti, &[], &[255])
                .unwrap()
                .flatten()
                .is_some()
        );
        let mut row = tx.get("entries", "r001").unwrap().unwrap();
        let old = row.clone();
        if let Value::Row(fields) = &mut row {
            fields.insert("text".into(), Value::String("changed".into()));
        }
        tx.put("entries", &schema.tables["entries"], &row, Some(&old))
            .unwrap();
        assert!(tx.activation_scan.as_ref().unwrap().borrow().is_none());
        tx.activation_head(t, ti, &[], &[255]).unwrap();
        let _ = tx.conn();
        assert!(tx.activation_scan.as_ref().unwrap().borrow().is_none());
        tx.activation_head(t, ti, &[], &[255]).unwrap();
        tx.delete("entries", &schema.tables["entries"], &row)
            .unwrap();
        assert!(tx.activation_scan.as_ref().unwrap().borrow().is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn raw_projection_and_savepoint_rollback_discard_buffered_images() {
        let (mut store, schema) = fixture();
        let mut tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        tx.activation_scan = Some(Default::default());
        let t = tx.num("entries").unwrap();
        let ti = tx.num("entries.byId").unwrap();
        let old = tx.get("entries", "r001").unwrap().unwrap();
        let mut changed = old.clone();
        if let Value::Row(fields) = &mut changed {
            fields.insert("text".into(), Value::String("temporary".into()));
        }
        tx.savepoint("before_projection").unwrap();
        tx.activation_head(t, ti, &[], &[255]).unwrap();
        // The same getter used by activation's raw projection/staging SQL.
        tx.conn().execute("UPDATE r SET data=?1 WHERE t=?2 AND id='r001'",
            params![canonical_json(&changed), t]).unwrap();
        assert!(tx.activation_scan.as_ref().unwrap().borrow().is_none());
        // Cache the changed r001 behind r000, then roll the physical write back.
        tx.activation_head(t, ti, &[], &[255]).unwrap();
        assert!(tx.activation_scan.as_ref().unwrap().borrow().is_some());
        tx.rollback_to("before_projection").unwrap();
        assert!(tx.activation_scan.as_ref().unwrap().borrow().is_none());
        let key = crate::rows::index_key(&schema.tables["entries"].indexes["byId"], &old, "r001");
        let (data, _) = tx.activation_head(t, ti, &key, &[255]).unwrap().unwrap().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(serde_json::from_str::<Value>(&data).unwrap(), old);
    }
}

impl Tx<'_> {
    pub(super) fn clear_activation_scan(&self) {
        if let Some(buffer) = &self.activation_scan {
            buffer.borrow_mut().take();
        }
    }

    /// None means use the ordinary adapter; Some(None) proves an empty range.
    /// No Value is decoded by read-ahead, so malformed/unvisited payloads do not
    /// change a caller's result or refusal. A too-wide first row uses the old path.
    pub(super) fn activation_head(
        &self,
        t: i64,
        ti: i64,
        lo: &[u8],
        hi: &[u8],
    ) -> Result<Option<Option<(String, Vec<u8>)>>, RuntimeError> {
        let Some(buffer) = &self.activation_scan else {
            return Ok(None);
        };
        let mut held = buffer.borrow_mut();
        if let Some(page) = held.as_mut() {
            if page.table == t && page.index == ti && page.hi == hi && lo >= page.lo.as_slice() {
                while page
                    .rows
                    .front()
                    .is_some_and(|(_, key)| key.as_slice() < lo)
                {
                    page.rows.pop_front();
                }
                if let Some(row) = page.rows.pop_front() {
                    // Only forward cursors may reuse the unread tail.
                    page.lo = crate::key::upper_bound(&row.1);
                    return Ok(Some(Some(row)));
                }
            }
        }
        held.take();
        const PAGE: usize = 32;
        const BYTES: u64 = 128 * 1024;
        let mut meter = Meter::new(
            Ceilings {
                heap_bytes: BYTES,
                ..Ceilings::job()
            },
            Cut::Abort,
        );
        meter.at("adopt serialized read-ahead");
        let fixed = heap::slots::<(String, Vec<u8>)>(PAGE)
            + heap::allocation(lo.len())
            + heap::allocation(hi.len())
            // upper_bound clones then appends: reserve the old cursor, clone,
            // and the Vec growth replacement together, including capacity.
            + 4 * heap::allocation(2 * crate::rows::MAX_INDEX_KEY_BYTES + 1)
            + 2 * self.page_size;
        // An unusually large SQLite page can simply disable this optimization.
        if !meter.room(crate::error::MeterKind::HeapBytes, fixed) {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        meter.heap(fixed)?;
        let mut count = 0;
        {
            let mut statement = self.tx.prepare_cached("SELECT octet_length(r.data), length(i.k) FROM i JOIN r ON r.t = ?1 AND r.id = i.id WHERE i.ti = ?2 AND i.k >= ?3 AND i.k < ?4 ORDER BY i.k LIMIT 32")?;
            let mut rows = statement.query(params![t, ti, lo, hi])?;
            while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                let bytes = 3
                    * (heap::allocation(row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize)
                        + heap::allocation(row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize))
                    + 64;
                if !meter.room(crate::error::MeterKind::HeapBytes, bytes) {
                    break;
                }
                meter.heap(bytes)?;
                meter.charge(crate::error::MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
                count += 1;
            }
        }
        if count == 0 {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let mut page = Page {
            table: t,
            index: ti,
            lo: lo.to_vec(),
            hi: hi.to_vec(),
            rows: VecDeque::with_capacity(PAGE),
            _meter: meter,
        };
        {
            let mut statement = self.tx.prepare_cached(scan_sql(Dir::Asc))?;
            let mut rows = statement.query(params![t, ti, lo, hi, count])?;
            while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
                page.rows.push_back((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?));
            }
        }
        let first = page.rows.pop_front();
        if let Some((_, key)) = &first {
            page.lo = crate::key::upper_bound(key);
        }
        *held = Some(page);
        Ok(Some(first))
    }
}

//! Timing-critical maintenance and bulk catch-up share bounded engine slices.
use super::{Shared, lock, refusal_json, respond};
use serde_json::json;
use snapback4_core::RuntimeError;
use std::{sync::Arc, time::{Duration, Instant}};
use tiny_http::Request;

#[derive(Default, serde::Serialize)]
struct Work { delivery_chunks: usize, expired: u64, schedules: usize, chunks: usize, parked: usize, #[serde(skip)] fired: Vec<String> }

const WORK_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_millis(2);
const CONTENDED_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_micros(250);

// Charge consumed CPU for pacing, not time the OS already kept this thread off
// CPU. The cooperative chunk deadline still uses wall time. A failed clock
// sample falls back to the more conservative wall-time pause.
struct SliceClock { wall: Instant, cpu: Option<Duration> }
impl SliceClock {
    fn start() -> Self { Self { wall: Instant::now(), cpu: Self::cpu_time() } }
    fn cpu_time() -> Option<Duration> {
        let mut at = libc::timespec { tv_sec: 0, tv_nsec: 0 };
        if unsafe { libc::clock_gettime(libc::CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID, &mut at) } != 0 { return None; }
        Some(Duration::new(at.tv_sec.try_into().ok()?, at.tv_nsec.try_into().ok()?))
    }
    fn consumed(&self) -> Duration {
        self.cpu.zip(Self::cpu_time()).and_then(|(start, end)| end.checked_sub(start)).unwrap_or_else(|| self.wall.elapsed())
    }
}

// Detached readers do not take engine tickets. Sample OS load as well as the
// round-3 engine contention signal; cache the syscall for 100 ms. Load includes
// other tenants and trails short bursts, so this is a pacing hint, not admission.
fn host_contended() -> bool {
    use std::sync::{Mutex, OnceLock};
    static SAMPLE: Mutex<(Option<Instant>, bool)> = Mutex::new((None, false));
    static CPUS: OnceLock<usize> = OnceLock::new();
    let mut sample = lock(&SAMPLE);
    if sample.0.is_none_or(|at| at.elapsed() >= Duration::from_millis(100)) {
        let mut load = 0.0;
        let cpus = *CPUS.get_or_init(|| std::thread::available_parallelism().map_or(1, |n| n.get()));
        sample.1 = unsafe { libc::getloadavg(&mut load, 1) } == 1 && load >= cpus as f64;
        sample.0 = Some(Instant::now());
    }
    sample.1
}

fn engine_work<T>(shared: &Shared, run: impl FnOnce(&mut snapback4_core::Engine, Duration) -> T) -> T {
    let mut engine = shared.engine.background();
    lock(&shared.readers).retry_missing(&engine);
    let budget = if engine.contended() || host_contended() { CONTENDED_TIME } else { WORK_TIME };
    let result = run(&mut engine, budget);
    // Publish from this guard: a second ordinary admission would both delay
    // notification and mistake maintenance for a newly arrived request.
    shared.publish_engine(&engine);
    result
}

fn phase<T: Default>(shared: &Shared, result: Result<T, RuntimeError>, drain: bool, name: &str) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
    match result {
        Err(error) if !drain => { super::adoption::background_failure(shared, name, &error); Ok(T::default()) }
        other => other,
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — lifetimes are swept into the partition log; missed schedule boundaries fire once.
fn work(shared: &Shared, drain: bool, tick: u64) -> Result<Work, RuntimeError> {
    work_at(shared, drain, tick, None)
}

fn work_at(shared: &Shared, drain: bool, tick: u64, schedule_sample: Option<i64>) -> Result<Work, RuntimeError> {
    let mut work = Work::default();
    shared.ephemeral.sweep(shared.now())?;
    work.delivery_chunks = delivery(shared, drain)?;
    loop {
        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_view_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "view recount")?.is_none() {break;}
        work.chunks += 1;
        if !drain && work.chunks >= 500 {break;}
        std::thread::yield_now();
    }
    loop {
        let swept = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.sweep_expired_for(shared.now(), 2_000, budget)), drain, "the expiry sweep")?;
        work.expired += swept;
        if !drain || swept == 0 { break; }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — advancing a replay clock does not release its schedule hold.
    if !shared.hold_schedules {
        let fired = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, _| {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.2 — select advance windows before draining; admission deadlines still use the live advanced clock.
            if let Some(sample) = schedule_sample {
                let windows = engine.schema().schedules.iter().enumerate().map(|(index, schedule)| (index, schedule.boundary(sample))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                let mut fired = Vec::new();
                for (index, boundary) in windows { fired.extend(engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(shared.now(), index, boundary)?); }
                Ok(fired)
            } else { engine.fire_schedules(shared.now()) }
        }), drain, "schedules")?;
        work.schedules = fired.len();
        for name in &fired { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: schedule {name} fired"); }
        work.fired = fired;
    }
    while drain || work.chunks < 500 {
        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
        let (run, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
            let run = engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget);
            (run, engine.take_native_logs())
        });
        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
        let Some(run) = phase(shared, run, drain, "job")? else { break; };
        work.chunks += 1;
        match run.state {
            snapback4_core::JobState::Done => snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} done: {} chunk{}, {} row{} written", run.name, run.chunks, if run.chunks == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, run.rows_written, if run.rows_written == 1 { "" } else { "s" }),
            snapback4_core::JobState::Parked => {
                work.parked += 1;
                if shared.personas { shared.refusals.job(&run); }
                snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} parked after {} chunks: {}", run.name, run.chunks, run.error.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
            }
            _ => {}
        }
        // FIFO engine admission hands waiting requests their turn before the
        // next chunk; yield_now alone cannot make that promise for a mutex.
    }
    if drain || tick % 5 == 0 {
        loop {
            let actions = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_actions_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the action lease sweep")?;
            let effects = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_effects_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the effect lease sweep")?;
            if actions + effects > 0 { shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine)); }
            if effects > 0 { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: {effects} effect lease{} expired; queued again", if effects == 1 { "" } else { "s" }); }
            if !drain || actions + effects == 0 { break; }
        }
    }
    if drain || tick % 60 == 0 {
        phase(shared, (|| {
            let mut sweep = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.prepare_asset_sweep_for(shared.now(), budget))?;
            sweep.remove_files()?;
            while !sweep.is_empty() { engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.finish_asset_sweep_for(&mut sweep, budget))?; }
            Ok(())
        })(), drain, "the asset sweep")?;
    }
    Ok(work)
}

fn delivery(shared: &Shared, drain: bool) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
    let mut chunks = 0;
    while drain || chunks < 500 {
        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_timeline_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "timeline delivery")?.is_none() { break; }
        chunks += 1;
    }
    Ok(chunks)
}

// @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — ordinary-priority bulk work yields
// outside every shared lock. Aged service protects finite backlogs from traffic.
const BULK_AGE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum BulkKind { Delivery, Recount, Job }
pub(super) struct State {
    next: BulkKind,
    serviced: Instant,
    resume_at: Instant,
    empty_pause: Duration,
    advancing: bool,
    // Separate clock callers (and a dev reset) may wait here; maintenance never takes this lock.
    pub(super) advance: Arc<std::sync::Mutex<()>>,
}
impl Default for State {
    fn default() -> Self {
        let now = Instant::now();
        Self { next: BulkKind::Delivery, serviced: now, resume_at: now, empty_pause: Duration::ZERO,
            advancing: false, advance: Arc::new(std::sync::Mutex::new(())) }
    }
}
impl State {
    fn aged(&self, now: Instant) -> bool { now.saturating_duration_since(self.serviced) >= BULK_AGE }
    fn eligible(&self, now: Instant, aged_only: bool) -> bool {
        !self.advancing && if aged_only { self.aged(now) } else { now >= self.resume_at }
    }
    fn completed(&mut self, now: Instant, elapsed: Duration, contended: bool, worked: bool) {
        self.next = match self.next { BulkKind::Delivery => BulkKind::Recount, BulkKind::Recount => BulkKind::Job, BulkKind::Job => BulkKind::Delivery };
        self.serviced = now;
        // Target 1/8 CPU duty under contention, charging consumed thread CPU,
        // including atomic overruns. Cap at 100 ms; aged service is independent.
        let cap = Duration::from_millis(100);
        let charged = if contended { (elapsed * 7).min(cap) } else { Duration::ZERO };
        self.empty_pause += charged;
        // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — empty kinds keep their CPU

        if self.device && predecessor.is_none() && has_children && table.is_expiring() && !minted {

                } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "parent identity has stored children") });

                (self.beyond_horizon, self.missing_fact) = (beyond, None);

            }

        // image as a fresh insert: an unchanged secondary key can now belong

        })?;

        self.row_changed(table_name, None, Some(&row), Origin::Source)?;
        Ok(true)
    }

    pub(super) fn update(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            runner.update_attempt(table_name, key, patch)
        })
    }

    fn update_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Update)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &patch, false)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &patch)?;
        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &patch)?;
        if patch.as_object()?.contains_key("id") {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} update patch cannot change id")));
        }
        let id = key.as_str()?;
        self.reserve_target_resolution(table_name, self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?, &patch, false)?;
        let Some(old) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| {
            runner.fetch_write_target(allowance, table_name, id)
        })? else {
            return Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()]));
        };
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].sync.is_none() { return Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()])); }
        let readable = !self.inactive_write_target(table_name, &old)?
            && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
        let patch = self.ignore_hidden_immutable_patch(table_name, patch, readable)?;
        let (new, copy) = self.merge_patch(table_name, &old, &patch, readable)?;
        self.replace(table_name, old, new, readable, &patch, copy)
    }

    pub(super) fn upsert(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            runner.upsert_attempt(table_name, key, row)
        })
    }

    fn upsert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_upsert_owned(table_name)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, false)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row)?;
        let id = key.as_str()?;
        if row.field("id")?.as_str()? != id {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} upsert row id must equal its key")));
        }
        self.reserve_target_resolution(table_name, self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?, &row, true)?;
        match self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| {
            runner.fetch_write_target(allowance, table_name, id)
        })? {
            Some(old) => {
                let readable = !self.inactive_write_target(table_name, &old)?
                    && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
                let row = self.ignore_hidden_immutable_patch(table_name, row, readable)?;
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — an existing row receives a patch; omitted columns keep their values.
                let (new, copy) = self.merge_patch(table_name, &old, &row, readable)?;
                self.replace(table_name, old, new, readable, &row, copy)
            }
            None if self.device && !self.upsert_absence_proven(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()])? =>
            {
                Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()]))
            }
            None => self.insert_upsert(table_name, "byId", row),
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn insert_upsert(&mut self, table_name: &str, addressed_index: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_upsert_owned(table_name)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#5 — trusted fixture imports preserve the constraint layer.
        if self.context.is_system() {
            return self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()).map_err(|error| match error {
                RuntimeError::Input(message) => RuntimeError::Constraint(message),
                other => other,
            });
        }
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            // A read-through child's insert arm keeps the hidden-target word for every authority refusal.
            return self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()).map_err(|e| match e {
                RuntimeError::Rule {..} | RuntimeError::Input(_) => self.no_write_target(), other => other,
            });
        }
        match self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()) {
            Err(RuntimeError::Input(_)) => Err(self.no_write_target()),
            result => result,
        }
    }

    fn replace(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value, new: Value, readable: bool, patch: &Value, copy: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.authorize_update_fields(table_name, &old, &new, patch, readable)?;
        self.replace_authorized(table_name, old, new, readable, patch, copy)
    }

    /// `copy` is the merged image's lawful price, charged again for an engine slot copy.
    pub(super) fn replace_authorized(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value, new: Value, readable: bool, patch: &Value, copy: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Update)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, patch, false)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, patch)?;
        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, patch)?;
        self.authorize_fields(table_name, &old, patch)?;
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        for path in definition.columns.keys().filter(|field| {
            definition.engine_field(field) && field.as_str() != crate::schema::through::DEADLINE
        }) {
            if old.field(path).ok() != new.field(path).ok() {
                return Err(if readable { RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_RECORDED_FIELD", message: format!("engine replacement changed stored attribution at {table_name}.{path} (guide maintains)") } } else { self.no_write_target() });
            }
        }
        let mut new = new;
        self.schema.tables[table_name].recompute_deadline(&mut new)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.017#2.2 — extending life re-admits the new image.
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].stamp_ephemeral(&mut new, self.context.now.unwrap_or(0))?
            && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &new)? {
            return Err(if readable { RuntimeError::Rule { table: table_name.into(), effect: Effect::Update } } else { self.no_write_target() });
        }
        let new = self.with_asset_facts(table_name, new, Some(&old), (!readable).then_some(patch))?;
        if let Err(error) = super::validate::validate_projection(self.schema, table_name, &new, self.device.then_some(self.context.viewer.as_str())) { return Err(if readable { error } else { self.no_write_target() }); }
        self.check_constraints(table_name, Some(&old), &new, Effect::Update)?;
        let new = self.prepare_event_slots(table_name, Some(&old), new, copy)?;
        // A predicting device keeps another owner's omitted private field as an omission.
        super::validate::validate_projection(self.schema, table_name, &new, self.device.then_some(self.context.viewer.as_str()))?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).expect("validated");
        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
            runner.tx.put(table_name, table, &new, Some(&old))
        })?;
        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(&old), Some(&new), entries)?;
        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(&old), Some(&new), Origin::Source)
    }

    pub(super) fn delete(&mut self, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| runner.delete_attempt(table_name, id))
    }

    fn delete_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_scope_delete(table_name)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        self.reserve_write_rules(table, &[Effect::Read, Effect::Delete])?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — delete prepays hidden contribution work before fetching its old image, with the same attempt lifetime as update.
        self.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
        let Some(old) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| {
            runner.fetch_write_target(allowance, table_name, id)
        })? else {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Require { site: Some(self.statement_site.clone()), code: "NOT_FOUND".to_owned(), message: None });
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — a delete that consults referencing rows needs read authority on its row first.
        let readable =
            !self.inactive_write_target(table_name, &old)? && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
        if (self.schema.children(table_name).next().is_some() || self.schema.is_referenced(table_name)) && !readable
        {
            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "read rule");
            return Err(self.no_write_target());
        }
        if !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Delete, None, &old)? {
            return Err(if readable {
                RuntimeError::Rule { table: table_name.to_owned(), effect: Effect::Delete }
            } else { self.no_write_target() });
        }
        self.delete_authorized(table_name, old)
    }

    pub(super) fn delete_authorized(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let old = if self.schema.is_referenced(table_name) {
            self.apply_reference_actions(table_name, &old)?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — the actions may have changed the root (a maintained column mirrored from its children): remove its current image.
            let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
            self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, &id))?.unwrap_or(old)
        } else { old };
        self.delete_row(table_name, old)
    }

    /// A delete's own removal of one row, its rules already decided: receipt
    /// membership, the read-through child probe, constraints, maintains and
    /// the partition event. A referential cascade removes each row this way.
    pub(super) fn delete_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        self.receipt_member_removed(table_name, &old)?;
        self.refuse_live_children(table_name,old.field("id")?)?;
        self.check_constraints(table_name, Some(&old), &old, Effect::Delete)?;
        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
            runner.tx.delete(table_name, table, &old)
        })?;
        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(&old), None, entries)?;
        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(&old), None, Origin::Source)
    }

    /// Remove a row the engine itself retires (an expired one): no rule and
    /// no constraint stands between the engine and its own sweep, while
    /// aggregates and partition events apply as for any delete.
    pub(super) fn remove(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
            runner.tx.delete(table_name, table, old)
        })?;
        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(old), None, entries)?;
        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(old), None, Origin::Source)
    }

    /// Physically retire one row whose upper lifetime ended. Expiry bypasses
    /// the root's authored delete rule and constraints, but it does not bypass
    /// the schema's declared effects on rows that reference the root. A
    /// read-through child still retains the expired parent physically.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — expiry cleanup runs the same closure/restrict/set-null actions as an authored delete.
    pub(crate) fn retire_expired_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.physical_children(table_name, old.field("id")?)? { return Ok(false); }
        let current = if self.schema.is_referenced(table_name) {
            self.apply_reference_actions(table_name, old)?;
            // A cascade may maintain a column on the root; delete its current
            // physical image so index and cap entries match what is stored.
            let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?;
            self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id))?
                .unwrap_or_else(|| old.clone())
        } else {
            old.clone()
        };
        self.remove(table_name, &current)?;
        Ok(true)
    }

    /// At a program commit's end, retire every source row the commit changed
    /// or inserted whose lifetime has already ended: its final
    /// image was never live at a commit boundary, so it leaves no row for a
    /// later sweep to announce. Inside the commit the
    /// row existed as written (an insert-then-update still works). Engine
    /// bookkeeping, unmetered: the program's metering is already recorded.
    /// Server execution and device prediction both run it so a predicted
    /// final image cannot omit cascade, set-null or restrict effects. Its work,
    /// returned, stays
    /// linear in the rows retired: each aggregate row takes one net change
    /// and each audience key is scanned for a live row once. Seeds and loads
    /// ordinarily keep their rows for the sweep; row-keyed view sources use
    /// `retire_seeded_view_sources` to preserve their count-basis contract.
    // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — a row whose lifetime ended by its own commit's end never exists at a commit boundary.
    pub(crate) fn retire_expired_births(&mut self) -> Result<crate::meter::Metered, RuntimeError> {
        let Some(now) = self.context.now else { return Ok(Default::default());
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.000.000#3 — an expired final image never grants a new recipient the row's id.
        // The tracker covers synced and unsynced source tables alike. Each
        // distinct entry prepaid its tree node, strings and this vector slot;
        // the rows-written ceiling bounds the commit-end walk.
        // A row that existed before the commit and ends it already expired is
        // retired too: accounted from the partitions it was in at the commit's
        // start, it revokes there and announces nothing to a partition it only
        // entered with its expiring image.
        let mut changed: Vec<(String, String)> = std::mem::take(&mut self.expiry_rows).into_iter().collect();
        if changed.is_empty() {
            return Ok(Default::default());
        }
        // The log's order must not depend on a map's iteration order.
        let order = self.schema.through_order();
        changed.sort_by_key(|(name,id)| {
            (std::cmp::Reverse(order.iter().position(|n| n == name)),id.clone(),
            )
        });
        // Server execution already reserved this bookkeeping in the source
        // writes. Device prediction instead runs the walk inside its separate
        // bounded physical-work meter: exhausting that allowance queues the
        // intent, never publishes a partial closure or refuses the write.
        let meter = if self.device_work_active { None } else {
            Some(std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, crate::meter::Meter::new(crate::meter::Ceilings::unbounded(), crate::meter::Cut::Abort)))
        };
        self.retiring = Some(Default::default());
        let retired = changed.iter().try_for_each(|(table_name, id)| {
            let Some(row) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id))? else { return Ok(());
            };
            let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
            if table.expired(&row, now) { self.retire_expired_row(table_name, &row).map(|_| ()) } else { Ok(()) }
        });
        let aggregates = self.retiring.take().map(|retiring| retiring.aggregates).unwrap_or_default();
        let retired = retired.and_then(|()| self.apply_retired_aggregates(aggregates));
        let work = meter.map(|meter| std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, meter).used).unwrap_or_default();
        retired.map(|()| work)
    }

    /// Seed writes historically retire only already-expired sources of
    /// row-keyed views. Keep that narrow boundary while routing their removal
    /// through the common action and net-maintenance path.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.5 — ordinary expired fixtures remain stored; derived row-view sources cannot revive after clock rollback.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(crate) fn retire_seeded_view_sources(&mut self) -> Result<crate::meter::Metered, RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        self.expiry_rows.retain(|(table, _)| schema.maintains.iter()
            .any(|maintain| maintain.source == table.as_str() && maintain.row_key.is_some()));
        self.retire_expired_births()
    }

fn completed_drain(work: Work, stopping: bool) -> Result<Work, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if stopping {
        return Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED", "clock", "clock advanced, but host shutdown interrupted the drain; inspect queued work instead of repeating the advance").guide("clock"));
    }
    Ok(work)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — staging grants clock control, never fixture or persona authority.
pub(super) fn staging(shared: &Shared) -> bool {
    lock(&shared.engine).store().meta("deployment_environment").ok().flatten().as_deref() == Some("staging")
}
fn clock_service(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<Option<String>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if shared.personas { return Ok(None); }
    if !staging(shared) { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
    match super::session_identity::principal_of(request,shared) {
        Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind == "service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Ok(Some(principal)),
        _ => Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "staging clock control requires a full service token").guide("clock")),
    }
}
pub(super) fn advance(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    let service = match clock_service(&request, shared) {
        Ok(service) => service,
        Err(why) => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})),
    };
    let payload = match serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(body) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))})),
    };
    let each_boundary = match payload.get("eachBoundary") {
        None => false,
        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => *value,
        Some(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary must be a boolean"))})),
    };
    let duration = payload.get("duration").and_then(|value| value.as_str());
    let milliseconds = match duration.ok_or_else(|| crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))
        .and_then(crate::dev_clock::duration) {
        Ok(milliseconds) => milliseconds,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // Serialize clock callers only. Background slices check advancing after
    // engine admission and skip during this synchronous drain.
    let advance = lock(&shared.maintenance).advance.clone();
    let _advance = lock(&advance);
    let _advancing = Advancing(shared);
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — simulated time makes retries due without stealing a live worker's claim or viewer session.
    let advanced = (|| {
        // Match claims' engine-then-clock lock order: no claim can land
        // between shifting its lease and publishing the advanced clock.
        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
        let before = crate::dev_clock_store::saved_boundaries(&engine);
        if let Some(principal) = &service {
            if engine.store().meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.refusal())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
            super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&engine,principal)?;
        } else { shared.dev_authority(&engine)?; }
        let mut clock = lock(&shared.clock);
        let mut next = clock.clone();
        let now = next.advance(milliseconds)?.max(shared.time_audience_clock.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire));
        let boundaries = crate::dev_clock_store::crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary)?;
        lock(&shared.maintenance).advancing = true;
        engine.advance_worker_leases_at(milliseconds, clock.now()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?;
        *clock = next;
        let offset = clock.offset();
        let skew = now - snapback4_core::now_ms();
        let isolated = if crate::dev_clock_store::has_data(&engine) {
            format!("; use {} for isolated time tests; clock advance --port N targets its printed port (guide jobs)", crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())
        } else { String::new() };
        let message = format!("cumulative offset {offset:+}ms (requested process offset; now includes the authoritative floor); process offset lasts until restart{isolated}");
        Ok((now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries))
    })();
    let (now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries) = match advanced {
        Ok(result) => result,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — a developer is awaiting this
    // request; synchronous clock-advance work intentionally keeps request QoS.
    let drained = (|| {
        let mut crossed = Vec::new();
        if each_boundary {
            for (index, at) in boundaries {
                crossed.extend(engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(shared.now(), index, at))?);
            }
        }
        let mut work = work_at(shared, true, 0, Some(now))?;
        work.schedules += crossed.len();
        work.fired.extend(crossed);
        Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(work)
    })();
    match drained.map_err(|error| error.refusal()).and_then(|work| completed_drain(work, crate::lifecycle::stopping())) {
        Ok(work) => {
            let schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&lock(&shared.engine), &before, &work.fired, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary, shared.dev_identity.as_ref().map(|i| i.address.port()));
            respond(request, 200, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "work":work, "schedules":schedules}))
        }
        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    }
}

        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
    };

pub fn serve_release(options: &Options) -> Result<(), String> {
    let requested_port = crate::port_flag::parse("serve", options.flags.get("port").map(String::as_str))?;
    let bind = release_bind(options)?;
    let capacity = crate::host_limits::Capacity::from_options(&options.flags, |name| {
        std::env::var(name).ok()
    })?;
    let dir = existing_deployment(&directory(options)?)?;
    crate::host_limits::initialize();
    let owner = crate::store_owner::host(&dir.join("store.sqlite"))?;
    owner.report_reclaim(options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
    let clock_offset = options.flags.get("clock-offset").map(|text| crate::dev_clock::offset(text).map_err(|e| e.to_string())).transpose()?.unwrap_or(0);
    if options.flags.contains_key("clock-offset") {
        let store = owner.open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        if store.meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release().to_string()); }
    }
    let current = current(&dir, &owner)?.ok_or("no current release; deploy first")?;
    let release = Project {
        root: dir.join("releases").join(current.to_string())
            .canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
    };
    crate::release_files::link_store(&release.root)?;
    let (port, listener) = crate::port_flag::listen("serve", requested_port, bind, None)?;
    let worker = options
        .flags
        .get("worker")
        .map(|path| {
            Path::new(path)
                .canonicalize()
                .map_err(|e| format!("--worker {path}: {e}"))
        })
        .transpose()?;
    crate::serve::serve(
        release,
        port,
        crate::serve::Mode::Release {
            clock_offset,
            expected: current,
            worker,
            owner: std::sync::Arc::new(owner),
        },
        &bind.to_string(),
        listener,
//! Private filesystem-owner control transport; never an HTTP application door.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5-hot-activation-and-ownership — the host remains the only database writer.
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
#[cfg(unix)]
use std::os::unix::{
    ffi::OsStrExt,
    fs::{DirBuilderExt, FileTypeExt, MetadataExt, OpenOptionsExt, PermissionsExt},
    net::{UnixListener, UnixStream},
};

    fs,
