const INPUT: usize = 4096;
const OUTPUT: usize = 64 * 1024;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(tag = "command", rename_all = "kebab-case", deny_unknown_fields)]
pub(crate) enum Command {
    Status {
        after: u64,
        items_after: Option<String>,
        event: Option<u64>,
    },
    Activate {
        release: u64,
        expected_release: Option<u64>,
        expected_revision: u64,
        digest: String,
        rollback: bool,
        restore_dormant: bool,
        allow_feed_adoption: bool,
    },
    Prune {
        confirm: bool,
        expected_revision: u64,
    },
}
#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Envelope {
    identity: String,
    command: Command,
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn identity(store: &Path) -> Result<String, String> {
    let path = store.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let m = snapback4_core::private_fs::database_metadata(&path)
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?
        .ok_or("store disappeared")?;
    Ok(crate::native::digest(
        &serde_json::to_vec(&(path.as_os_str().as_bytes(), m.dev(), m.ino())).unwrap(),
    ))
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn peer(stream: &UnixStream) -> io::Result<()> {
    use std::os::fd::AsRawFd;
    #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
    let uid = {
        let mut credentials = std::mem::MaybeUninit::<libc::ucred>::uninit();
        let mut size = std::mem::size_of::<libc::ucred>() as libc::socklen_t;
        if unsafe {
            libc::getsockopt(
                stream.as_raw_fd(),
                libc::SOL_SOCKET,
                libc::SO_PEERCRED,
                credentials.as_mut_ptr().cast(),
                &mut size,
            )
        } != 0
        {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        if size as usize != std::mem::size_of::<libc::ucred>() {
            return Err(io::Error::other("invalid peer credentials"));
        }
        unsafe { credentials.assume_init() }.uid
    };
    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
    let uid = {
        let (mut uid, mut gid) = (0, 0);
        if unsafe { libc::getpeereid(stream.as_raw_fd(), &mut uid, &mut gid) } != 0 {
            return Err(io::Error::last_os_error());
        }
        uid
    };
    #[cfg(not(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos")))]
    return Err(io::Error::other(
        "release control peer credentials unsupported on this Unix host",
    ));
    #[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", target_os = "macos"))]
    if uid != unsafe { libc::geteuid() } {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control requires the same filesystem owner",
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn socket_entry(path: &Path) -> io::Result<fs::Metadata> {
    let m = fs::symlink_metadata(path)?;
    if !m.file_type().is_socket()
        || m.uid() != unsafe { libc::geteuid() }
        || m.permissions().mode() & 0o7777 != 0o600
    {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control must be a same-owner 0600 socket, never a symlink or regular file",
        ));
    }
    Ok(m)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#51-path-independent-control-address — fixed short runtime root, independent of shell environment.
#[cfg(unix)]
fn private_directory(path: &Path, create: bool) -> io::Result<()> {
    if create {
        match fs::DirBuilder::new().mode(0o700).create(path) {
            Ok(()) => {}
            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists => {}
            Err(e) => return Err(e),
        }
    }
    // Do not chmod or follow an existing entry. A stranger can pre-create a
    // name in /tmp, but cannot turn that into a capability or chmod target.
    let directory = fs::OpenOptions::new()
        .read(true)
        .custom_flags(libc::O_DIRECTORY | libc::O_NOFOLLOW)
        .open(path)?;
    directory_metadata(&directory.metadata()?, unsafe { libc::geteuid() })
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn directory_metadata(m: &fs::Metadata, uid: u32) -> io::Result<()> {
    if !m.is_dir() || m.uid() != uid || m.permissions().mode() & 0o7777 != 0o700 {
        return Err(io::Error::other(
            "release control runtime directory must be a same-owner 0700 directory",
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(unix)]
fn address(dir: &Path, create: bool) -> io::Result<PathBuf> {
    let directory = PathBuf::from(format!("/tmp/snapback4-{}", unsafe { libc::geteuid() }));
    private_directory(&directory, create)?;
    let canonical = dir.canonicalize()?;
    // Bind the address to immutable deployment source, even when two
    // deployments symlink their database to the same store. The actual
    // database path/inode is separately checked in every envelope.
    Ok(directory.join(format!(
        "{}.sock",
        crate::native::digest(canonical.as_os_str().as_bytes())
    )))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#51-path-independent-control-address — a total input deadline bounds shutdown even when a peer keeps sending bytes.
#[cfg(unix)]
fn input(stream: &mut UnixStream, timeout: Duration) -> io::Result<Vec<u8>> {
    let deadline = Instant::now() + timeout;
    let mut bytes = Vec::new();
    let mut buffer = [0; INPUT + 1];
    while bytes.len() <= INPUT {
        let remaining = deadline.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now());
        if remaining.is_zero() {
            return Err(io::Error::new(
                io::ErrorKind::TimedOut,
                "release control input deadline exceeded",
            ));
        }
        stream.set_read_timeout(Some(remaining))?;
        match stream.read(&mut buffer[..INPUT + 1 - bytes.len()]) {
            Ok(0) => break,
            Ok(n) => bytes.extend_from_slice(&buffer[..n]),
            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::Interrupted => continue,
            Err(e) => return Err(e),
        }
    }
    Ok(bytes)
}

/// Only called while the exclusive database sidecar is held, so stale socket
/// replacement cannot disconnect another admitted serving owner.
#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) struct Listener {
    listener: UnixListener,
    path: PathBuf,
    inode: (u64, u64),
    identity: String,
}
#[cfg(unix)]
impl Listener {
    pub(crate) fn bind(dir: &Path, store: &Path) -> Result<Self, String> {
        let identity = identity(store)?;
        let path = address(dir, true).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        match fs::symlink_metadata(&path) {
            Ok(_) => {
                socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
                match UnixStream::connect(&path) {
                    Ok(_) => {
                        return Err("release control socket already has a live listener".into());
                    }
                    Err(e)
                        if matches!(
                            e.kind(),
                            io::ErrorKind::ConnectionRefused | io::ErrorKind::NotFound
                        ) => {}
                    Err(e) => return Err(e.to_string()),
                }
                fs::remove_file(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            }
            Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {}
            Err(e) => return Err(e.to_string()),
        }
        let listener = UnixListener::bind(&path)
            .map_err(|e| format!("release control {}: {e}", path.display()))?;
        let m = fs::symlink_metadata(&path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        let owned = Self {
            listener,
            path,
            inode: (m.dev(), m.ino()),
            identity,
        };
        // Establish cleanup custody before any fallible setup after bind.
        fs::set_permissions(&owned.path, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600))
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        owned
            .listener
            .set_nonblocking(true)
            .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        socket_entry(&owned.path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
        Ok(owned)
    }
    fn accept(&self) -> io::Result<UnixStream> {
        let (stream, _) = self.listener.accept()?;
        // Darwin inherits O_NONBLOCK from the listener; input's deadline and
        // socket timeouts require blocking I/O, even before the client writes.
        stream.set_nonblocking(false)?;
        Ok(stream)
    }
    fn handle(
        &self,
        stream: &mut UnixStream,
        mut handle: impl FnMut(Command) -> Result<Value, String>,
    ) {
        let result = (|| -> Result<Value, String> {
            peer(&stream).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            stream
                .set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5)))
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            let bytes = input(stream, Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if bytes.len() > INPUT {
                return Err("release control command exceeds 4096 bytes".into());
            }
            let request: Envelope = serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            if request.identity != self.identity {
                return Err("release control store identity differs; run releases against the owned deployment".into());
            }
            handle(request.command)
        })();
        let reply = match result {
            Ok(value) => json!({"ok":value}),
            Err(error) => json!({"error":error}),
        };
        let mut bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&reply).unwrap();
        if bytes.len() > OUTPUT {
            bytes=serde_json::to_vec(&json!({"error":"release control reply exceeds its bound; inspect paginated `snapback4 releases <dir>`; an admitted activation/prune may already have committed"})).unwrap();
        }
        // A timeout/lost peer does not cancel or undo admitted work.
        let _ = stream.write_all(&bytes);
    }
    pub(crate) fn run(self, mut handle: impl FnMut(Command) -> Result<Value, String>) {
        while !crate::lifecycle::stopping() {
            match self.accept() {
                Ok(mut stream) => {
                    self.handle(&mut stream, &mut handle);
                }
                Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock => {
                    std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(25))
                }
                Err(e) => {
                    snapback4_core::diagnostic!("release control accept: {e}");
                    std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50));
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
#[cfg(unix)]
impl Drop for Listener {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if fs::symlink_metadata(&self.path)
            .is_ok_and(|m| m.file_type().is_socket() && (m.dev(), m.ino()) == self.inode)
        {
            let _ = fs::remove_file(&self.path);
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(unix)]
pub(crate) fn request(dir: &Path, command: Command) -> Result<Value, String> {
    let store = dir.join("store.sqlite");
    let unavailable = |reason: String| {
        let owner_path = store.canonicalize().unwrap_or_else(|_| store.clone());
        let detail = if reason.contains("No such file or directory") {
            "this deployment has no release control socket".to_owned()
        } else {
            reason
        };
        format!(
            "{}; release control unavailable: {detail}; stop `snapback4 serve {}`, rerun the exact command, then `snapback4 serve {}`",
            crate::store_owner::busy(&owner_path),
            dir.display(),
            dir.display()
        )
    };
    let path = address(dir, false).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let before = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let mut stream = UnixStream::connect(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    peer(&stream).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let after = socket_entry(&path).map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    if (before.dev(), before.ino()) != (after.dev(), after.ino()) {
        return Err(unavailable(
            "release control socket changed before send".into(),
        ));
    }
    stream
        .set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(120)))
        .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    stream
        .set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5)))
        .map_err(|e| unavailable(e.to_string()))?;
    let bytes = serde_json::to_vec(&Envelope {
        identity: identity(&store)?,
        command,
    })
    .unwrap();
    if bytes.len() > INPUT {
        return Err("release control command exceeds 4096 bytes".into());
    }
    let ambiguous = |reason: io::Error| {
        format!(
            "release command result is unknown: {reason}; run `snapback4 releases {}` to inspect database authority; the server continues admitted work. Do not automatically deploy another release",
            dir.display()
        )
    };
    stream.write_all(&bytes).map_err(ambiguous)?;
    stream
        .shutdown(std::net::Shutdown::Write)
        .map_err(ambiguous)?;
    let mut reply = Vec::new();
    stream
        .take((OUTPUT + 1) as u64)
        .read_to_end(&mut reply)
        .map_err(ambiguous)?;
    if reply.len() > OUTPUT {
        return Err("release reply too large; inspect releases before retrying".into());
    }
    let value: Value = serde_json::from_slice(&reply).map_err(|e| {
        format!(
            "release result is unknown ({e}); inspect `snapback4 releases {}` before retrying",
            dir.display()
        )
    })?;
    match value.get("ok") {
        Some(value) => Ok(value.clone()),
        None => Err(value["error"]
            .as_str()
            .unwrap_or("release control refused command; inspect releases")
            .into()),
    }
}

#[cfg(not(unix))]
pub(crate) fn request(_: &Path, _: Command) -> Result<Value, String> {
    Err("local release control requires Unix".into())
}

#[cfg(all(test, unix))]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::sync::{
        atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering},
        mpsc,
    };
    static NEXT: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
    struct Directory(PathBuf);
    impl Directory {
        fn new(long: bool) -> Self {
            let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
                "sb4-roll-control-{}-{}",
                std::process::id(),
                NEXT.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed)
            ));
            let root = if long {
                root.join("long-path-component".repeat(6))
            } else {
                root
            };
            snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&root).unwrap();
            let _store = snapback4_core::Store::open(&root.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
            Self(root)
        }
    }
    impl Drop for Directory {
        fn drop(&mut self) {
            let _ = fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);
        }
    }
    #[test]
    fn socket_permissions_and_entry_types_are_enforced() {
        let dir = Directory::new(false);
        let path = address(&dir.0, true).unwrap();
        fs::write(&path, "not a socket").unwrap();
        assert!(
            Listener::bind(&dir.0, &dir.0.join("store.sqlite"))
                .err()
                .unwrap()
                .contains("0600 socket")
        );
        fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink("store.sqlite", &path).unwrap();
        assert!(Listener::bind(&dir.0, &dir.0.join("store.sqlite")).is_err());
        fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
        let listener = Listener::bind(&dir.0, &dir.0.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(
            fs::metadata(&path).unwrap().permissions().mode() & 0o777,
            0o600
        );
        for mode in [0o666, 0o1600] {
            fs::set_permissions(&path, fs::Permissions::from_mode(mode)).unwrap();
            assert!(
                request(
                    &dir.0,
                    Command::Status {
                        after: 0,
                        items_after: None,
                        event: None
                    }
                )
                .unwrap_err()
                .contains("0600 socket")
            );
        }
        drop(listener);
        assert!(!path.exists());
    }
    #[test]
    fn accepted_nonblocking_listener_waits_for_delayed_client_input() {
        let dir = Directory::new(false);
        let listener = Listener::bind(&dir.0, &dir.0.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
        let path = listener.path.clone();
        let envelope = Envelope {
            identity: listener.identity.clone(),
            command: Command::Status { after: 0, items_after: None, event: None },
        };
        // Connect before starting accept so the listener stays nonblocking.
        let mut client = UnixStream::connect(&path).unwrap();
        client.set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(10))).unwrap();
        client.set_write_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(10))).unwrap();
        let (accepted, waiting) = mpsc::channel();
        let task = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let mut stream = listener.accept().unwrap();
            accepted.send(()).unwrap();
            listener.handle(&mut stream, |command| {
                assert!(matches!(command, Command::Status { after: 0, .. }));
                Ok(json!({"delayed":true}))
            });
        });
        waiting.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(10)).unwrap();
        // Without normalization Darwin reads WouldBlock and closes the peer
        // here. Linux accept does not inherit O_NONBLOCK, so it cannot expose
        // the unfixed platform bug with this test.
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(100));
        client.write_all(&serde_json::to_vec(&envelope).unwrap()).unwrap();
        client.shutdown(std::net::Shutdown::Write).unwrap();
        let mut reply = String::new();
        client.read_to_string(&mut reply).unwrap();
        task.join().unwrap();
        assert_eq!(serde_json::from_str::<Value>(&reply).unwrap(), json!({"ok":{"delayed":true}}));
    }
    #[test]
    fn input_deadline_expires_while_the_peer_keeps_sending() {
        let (mut reader, mut writer) = UnixStream::pair().unwrap();
        let task = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            while writer.write_all(b" ").is_ok() {
                std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20));
            }
        });
        let started = Instant::now();
        let error = input(&mut reader, Duration::from_millis(300)).unwrap_err();
        assert!(
            matches!(
                error.kind(),
                io::ErrorKind::TimedOut | io::ErrorKind::WouldBlock
            ),
            "{error}"
        );
        assert!(
            started.elapsed() < Duration::from_secs(2),
            "input deadline reset on each read"
        );
        drop(reader);
        task.join().unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn runtime_directory_refuses_permissions_symlinks_files_and_wrong_owner() {
        let dir = Directory::new(false);
        let runtime = dir.0.join("runtime");
        private_directory(&runtime, true).unwrap();
        private_directory(&runtime, false).unwrap();
        let m = fs::metadata(&runtime).unwrap();
        assert!(directory_metadata(&m, m.uid().wrapping_add(1)).is_err());
        for mode in [0o755, 0o777, 0o1700] {
            fs::set_permissions(&runtime, fs::Permissions::from_mode(mode)).unwrap();
            assert!(private_directory(&runtime, true).is_err());
            assert!(private_directory(&runtime, false).is_err());
            assert_eq!(fs::metadata(&runtime).unwrap().mode() & 0o7777, mode);
        }
        fs::remove_dir(&runtime).unwrap();
        fs::write(&runtime, "untouched").unwrap();
        assert!(private_directory(&runtime, true).is_err());
        assert_eq!(fs::read(&runtime).unwrap(), b"untouched");
        fs::remove_file(&runtime).unwrap();
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&dir.0, &runtime).unwrap();
        assert!(private_directory(&runtime, true).is_err());
    }

    #[test]
    fn stale_socket_is_replaced_but_a_live_listener_is_preserved() {
        let dir = Directory::new(false);
        let store = dir.0.join("store.sqlite");
        let path = address(&dir.0, true).unwrap();
        let stale = UnixListener::bind(&path).unwrap();
        fs::set_permissions(&path, fs::Permissions::from_mode(0o600)).unwrap();
        drop(stale); // Process death leaves the socket entry behind.
        let live = Listener::bind(&dir.0, &store).unwrap();
        let inode = live.inode;
        assert!(
            Listener::bind(&dir.0, &store)
                .err()
                .unwrap()
                .contains("live listener")
        );
        let m = fs::symlink_metadata(&path).unwrap();
        assert_eq!((m.dev(), m.ino()), inode);
        drop(live);
        assert!(!path.exists());
    }

    #[test]
    fn cleanup_preserves_a_replacement_and_canonical_aliases_share_an_address() {
        let dir = Directory::new(false);
        let other = Directory::new(false);
        let store = dir.0.join("store.sqlite");
        let listener = Listener::bind(&dir.0, &store).unwrap();
        let path = listener.path.clone();
        assert_ne!(path, address(&other.0, true).unwrap());
        let alias = dir.0.join("alias");
        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&dir.0, &alias).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(path, address(&alias, false).unwrap());
        assert!(path.as_os_str().as_bytes().len() < 104);
        fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
        fs::write(&path, "replacement").unwrap();
        drop(listener);
        assert_eq!(fs::read(&path).unwrap(), b"replacement");
        fs::remove_file(&path).unwrap();
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejected_store_identity_does_not_execute_command() {
        let dir = Directory::new(false);
        let listener = Listener::bind(&dir.0, &dir.0.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
        listener.listener.set_nonblocking(false).unwrap();
        let task = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let (mut stream, _) = listener.listener.accept().unwrap();
            listener.handle(&mut stream, |_| panic!("wrong identity admitted"));
        });
        let mut stream = UnixStream::connect(address(&dir.0, true).unwrap()).unwrap();
        stream.write_all(br#"{"identity":"wrong","command":{"command":"status","after":0,"items_after":null,"event":null}}"#).unwrap();
        stream.shutdown(std::net::Shutdown::Write).unwrap();
        let mut reply = String::new();
        stream.read_to_string(&mut reply).unwrap();
        assert!(reply.contains("identity differs"));
        task.join().unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn lost_reply_does_not_cancel_admitted_command() {
        let dir = Directory::new(false);
        let listener = Listener::bind(&dir.0, &dir.0.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
        listener.listener.set_nonblocking(false).unwrap();
        let (entered, waiting) = mpsc::channel();
        let (resume, resumed) = mpsc::channel();
        let (done, finished) = mpsc::channel();
        let task = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let (mut stream, _) = listener.listener.accept().unwrap();
            listener.handle(&mut stream, |_| {
                entered.send(()).unwrap();
                resumed.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(10)).unwrap();
                done.send(()).unwrap();
                Ok(json!({"committed":true}))
            });
        });
        let mut stream = UnixStream::connect(address(&dir.0, true).unwrap()).unwrap();
        let envelope = Envelope {
            identity: identity(&dir.0.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap(),
            command: Command::Prune {
                confirm: true,
                expected_revision: 1,
            },
        };
        stream
            .write_all(&serde_json::to_vec(&envelope).unwrap())
            .unwrap();
        stream.shutdown(std::net::Shutdown::Write).unwrap();
        waiting.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(10)).unwrap();
        drop(stream);
        resume.send(()).unwrap();
        finished.recv_timeout(Duration::from_secs(10)).unwrap();
        task.join().unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn long_deployment_path_uses_same_private_socket() {
        let dir = Directory::new(true);
        assert!(dir.0.as_os_str().as_bytes().len() > 108);
        let listener = Listener::bind(&dir.0, &dir.0.join("store.sqlite")).unwrap();
        listener.listener.set_nonblocking(false).unwrap();
        let task = std::thread::spawn(move || {
            let (mut stream, _) = listener.listener.accept().unwrap();
            listener.handle(&mut stream, |_| Ok(json!({"release":2})));
        });
        assert_eq!(
            request(
                &dir.0,
                Command::Status {
                    after: 0,
                    items_after: None,
                    event: None
                }
            )
            .unwrap()["release"],
            2
        );
        task.join().unwrap();
        assert!(!address(&dir.0, true).unwrap().exists());
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — staging grants clock control, never fixture or persona authority.
pub(super) fn staging(shared: &Shared) -> bool {
    lock(&shared.engine).store().meta("deployment_environment").ok().flatten().as_deref() == Some("staging")
}
fn clock_service(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<Option<String>, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if shared.personas { return Ok(None); }
    if !staging(shared) { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
    match super::session_identity::principal_of(request,shared) {
        Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind == "service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Ok(Some(principal)),
        _ => Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "staging clock control requires a full service token").guide("clock")),
    }
}
pub(super) fn advance(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    let service = match clock_service(&request, shared) {
        Ok(service) => service,
        Err(why) => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})),
    };
    let payload = match serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(body) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))})),
    };
    let each_boundary = match payload.get("eachBoundary") {
        None => false,
        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => *value,
        Some(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary must be a boolean"))})),
    };
    let duration = payload.get("duration").and_then(|value| value.as_str());
    let milliseconds = match duration.ok_or_else(|| crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))
        .and_then(crate::dev_clock::duration) {
        Ok(milliseconds) => milliseconds,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // Serialize clock callers only. Background slices check advancing after
    // engine admission and skip during this synchronous drain.
    let advance = lock(&shared.maintenance).advance.clone();
    let _advance = lock(&advance);
    let _advancing = Advancing(shared);
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — simulated time makes retries due without stealing a live worker's claim or viewer session.
    let advanced = (|| {
        // Match claims' engine-then-clock lock order: no claim can land
        // between shifting its lease and publishing the advanced clock.
        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
        let before = crate::dev_clock_store::saved_boundaries(&engine);
        if let Some(principal) = &service {
            if engine.store().meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.refusal())?.as_deref() != Some("staging") { return Err(crate::dev_clock::release()); }
            super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&engine,principal)?;
        } else { shared.dev_authority(&engine)?; }
        let mut clock = lock(&shared.clock);
        let mut next = clock.clone();
        let now = next.advance(milliseconds)?.max(shared.time_audience_clock.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire));
        let boundaries = crate::dev_clock_store::crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary)?;
        lock(&shared.maintenance).advancing = true;
        engine.advance_worker_leases_at(milliseconds, clock.now()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?;
        *clock = next;
        let offset = clock.offset();
        let skew = now - snapback4_core::now_ms();
        let isolated = if crate::dev_clock_store::has_data(&engine) {
            format!("; use {} for isolated time tests; clock advance --port N targets its printed port (guide jobs)", crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())
        } else { String::new() };
        let message = format!("cumulative offset {offset:+}ms (requested process offset; now includes the authoritative floor); process offset lasts until restart{isolated}");
        Ok((now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries))
    })();
    let (now, offset, skew, message, before, boundaries) = match advanced {
        Ok(result) => result,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — a developer is awaiting this
    // request; synchronous clock-advance work intentionally keeps request QoS.
    let drained = (|| {
        let mut crossed = Vec::new();
        if each_boundary {
            for (index, at) in boundaries {
                crossed.extend(engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(shared.now(), index, at))?);
            }
        }
        let mut work = work_at(shared, true, 0, Some(now))?;
        work.schedules += crossed.len();
        work.fired.extend(crossed);
        Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(work)
    })();
    match drained.map_err(|error| error.refusal()).and_then(|work| completed_drain(work, crate::lifecycle::stopping())) {
        Ok(work) => {
            let schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&lock(&shared.engine), &before, &work.fired, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary, shared.dev_identity.as_ref().map(|i| i.address.port()));
            respond(request, 200, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "work":work, "schedules":schedules}))
        }
        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":skew, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    }
}

/// `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}`: queue one run of a job as the
/// system on the dev clock and drive chunks until it is done or parked. This is
/// what `snapback4 run <job>` forwards to while dev owns the store.
pub(super) fn run_job(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
    use snapback4_core::{JobState, error::Refusal, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
    let service_route=request.url().starts_with("/jobs/");
    let service=if service_route {
        match super::session_identity::principal_of(&request,shared) {
            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind=="service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Some(principal),
            _ => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","triggering a deployed system job requires a full service token").guide("jobs"))})),
        }
    } else {
        if !shared.personas {return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","/dev/jobs is a development door; use run <job> --release <dir> with a service token").guide("jobs"))}));}
        None
    };
    let refuse = |request, status, refusal: Refusal| respond(request, status, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal.guide("jobs"))}));
    let payload: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(if body.trim().is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
    };
    if service_route && !shared.personas {crate::release_requests::Scope::admit(&shared.release_requests,||*lock(&shared.generation));}
    let program = lock(&shared.bound).programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == name).map(|p| p.program.clone());
    let program = match program {
        Some(program) if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job => program,
        Some(other) => return refuse(request, 404, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{name} is a {}, not a job; queries are POST /q/<op>, mutations POST /m/<op>", other.kind.word()))),
        None => return respond(request, 404, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&super::dev_doors::stale(shared, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown job {name}")).guide("jobs")))})),
    };
    if let Some(raw)=payload.get("args") && let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,raw) {return refuse(request,400,why);}
    let mut args = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
    if let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object) {
        for (field, value) in fields {
            let Some(kind) = program.args.get(field) else { return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{name} takes no argument {field}"))); };
            match wire::from_json(kind, value) {
                Ok(typed) => { args.insert(field.clone(), typed); }
                Err(why) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {field}: {why}"))),
            }

# LLP 3000.000.000: Retained storage and hot release activation

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Schema evolution, Storage, CLI, Deployment, Replica generations
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (Workstream ROLL)
**Date:** 2026-10-12
**Revised:** 2026-10-18 (DAY5 wave-15 amendments); 2026-10-16 (PREV wave-13 preview decisions §13); 2026-10-15 (SURF readiness/closeout; aggregate identity migration covers retained references; PUBFIX28: consistent disjoint preview prose and blocking accepted control streams); 2026-10-14 (NARR wave-11 amendment); 2026-10-13 (§10: REL2 wave-10 release lifecycle); 2026-10-13 (MACFIX: explicit pre-retention compatibility boundary and canonical deployment/worker paths); 2026-10-12 (§5.1: path-independent control socket; implemented; dual-family confirmation, captured lifetimes, native generations, hot activation and complete qualification)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md), [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.019](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md)

Staging store classification and real-user provisioning: [LLP 3000.000.000.001](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md) (STAGE).

## 1. Commission and scope

Charlie approved the ideal end-game release lifecycle and commissioned implementation on 2026-10-12. Implementer: this ROLL session, beginning that date. Wave 8 DT1 found additive rollback deletes new storage in all 50 day-two lanes; DT1a found unnamed enum conflicts; DT10 found deployment requires stopping serve. This supersedes the release-only removal semantics in LLP 3000.000's implementation follow-up. Development and device replacement keep their existing projection/reset semantics. No new publication authority or gate is introduced.

## 2. Retain by default

Every release activation (deploy and rollback) retains removed durable application tables, columns and index definitions in SQLite-owned dormant storage within the same activation transaction. The active r/i image remains an exact projection of the active schema. Dormant data is outside that namespace and unavailable to programs, rules, references, queries, sync, SQL application projections and clients. The Quarry/IR binder admits only declared application table names; it has no general application SQL evaluator. Dormant internal names are not added to Schema, and schema validation forbids declarations in the reserved `_dormant_` and `_release_` namespaces. Inspection `snapback4 sql` is owner-only and may enumerate it. A future application SQL adapter must keep its declared authorized input allow-list. Asset reads must resolve the home table, column and row in the active schema/r image and check the current read rule; a dormant home refuses E_ASSET_UNKNOWN even when a caller knows its id. Test the reserved-name/program boundary and direct fetch by id. Reintroducing a dormant name deliberately reactivates its data, after full validation under the candidate schema. A deployment that reuses any dormant name refuses unless --restore-dormant explicitly selects restoration; its refusal lists retained items/counts/samples and the alternatives `deploy <dir> --restore-dormant` or `prune <dir> --allow-destructive` before fresh creation. Rollback's selection of an immutable older release explicitly selects restoration; `rollback <dir> --to N` also selects an immutable release for deliberate roll forward. To convert durable to ephemeral, deploy a release without that table, prune it explicitly, then deploy the ephemeral definition. This applies to deployments as well as rollback; a rename remains two names, without an inferred data move.

Inventory and audit entries carry a stable typed key (`table:name`, `column:table.name`, or `index:table.name`); pagination uses the returned key, not a bare path that could collide between columns and indexes. The SQLite format is `_dormant_tables(table_name, definition)` for whole-table Table definitions, `_dormant_rows(t,id,data)` for their stored images, `_dormant_columns(table_name,column_name,definition)` for retired column type/default/ownership, `_dormant_values(t,id,column_name,data)` for their cells, `_dormant_indexes(table_name,index_name,definition)` for index definitions and `_dormant_refs(t,id,column_name,parent_t,parent_id,invalid)` for deletion evidence with a parent lookup index. Catalog numbers are reserved monotonically and reused for the same physical name, but dormant rows are never kept in r under a hidden catalog number. These tables are created only during successful release activation; legacy deployments start empty and cannot recover data previously deleted by older binaries. A successful release activation writes the release-only format marker `version=snapback4-release-store-3`. New Store open/version checks accept this, the prior snapback4-release-store-2 and the legacy snapback4-store-1; old binaries reject it with E_STORE rather than opening it. Development and device stores retain snapback4-store-1 and their formats are unchanged. The format marker rolls back with failed activation. On restore, omitted cells from whole-table images move to dormant column cells before projection, merging previous retired cells; full-table entries are consumed only on commit.

The compatibility boundary is the store format, independently of schema
capabilities. Published 0.2.33 refuses retained release-store versions 2 and 3
with `E_STORE: store version <version> is not snapback4-store-1; this data
directory belongs to another Snapback` (the read-only doctor form omits
the final data-directory explanation), including capability-free releases.
That refusal must preserve committed state and never interpret dormant data
under the old layout. Capability-free backend/schema bytes and unchanged
store-1 development/device fixtures retain their separate compatibility
controls (LLP 3000.022 §14.3); they do not establish old-server readability of
new release stores. The Darwin publication fence checks both boundaries.
The MACFIX compatibility/path amendment passed independent final
[Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000-macfix-r3.muse.md) and
[Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000-macfix-r3.codex.md) reviews at e9150bd171.
The required Darwin schema and worker files passed alone with default TMPDIR
(10 and 82 tests); whole-publication qualification is a separate claim.

Dormant whole tables store their row images; dormant columns store values keyed by table/column/row id and their definitions. Capture interpreted literal defaults and optional nulls when a column becomes dormant, preserving the value existing rows actually saw. Keep index definitions as history; preserve their data logically and rebuild physical keys on reactivation from current rows. Dormant indexes are never consulted for active writes, uniqueness or caps. Thus a new duplicate under the older release can precisely refuse forward activation. Derived rows are caches and are rebuilt, not authoritative history. Retention must precede existing derived-state retirement. An unsupported durable-to-ephemeral conversion refuses rather than silently discarding stored data.

Partial restoration consumes only candidate-visible cells: B's T(a,b) → absent T → C's T(a) restores a and moves b to dormant cells, with b's definition, then B restores b. For an identical (t,id,column) cell key, the newly retired active value wins: it is the most recent authoritative value. Preserve other dormant keys. Use transactional upsert and test retire → restore → update → retire → restore. Keep zero-row table/column/index definitions too. Whole-table restoration first restores its stored image, splits omitted cells into dormancy, then projects the active image. Consumption shares commit with validation. Refuse application-table to derived-table ownership changes, or authoritative-column to maintained/captured ownership changes, including when the application table/column was dormant. Derived caches never overwrite retained authoritative data. The refusal names the incompatible owner and directs a deliberate rename or prune while dormant.

At retirement record reference edges for dormant rows/cells (including optional/list references), bound to their current parent lifetime. An active delete transaction marks matching dormant edges invalid before the id can be reused, even for identical replacement values. Only a move into dormant storage or replacement of a derived cache is exempt from deletion invalidation. Actual authoritative-row deletion always invalidates matching dormant edges, including expiry during activation; failed activation rolls the invalidation back. A fixed-size identity migration rewrites dormant whole rows, cells, narrowed rows and their edge ledger when the referenced derived target lifetime survives migration; removing that target retires its lifetime, so its dormant edges remain bound to the retired address and restoration refuses rather than attaching them to a later reintroduction. Restoration refuses invalidated edges or missing parents with table/column/parent ids, counts and samples; it cannot silently recapture another parent's audience. For read-through rows, compare preserved captured audience and identity fields to the candidate parent before existing recapture work. A changed audience refuses restoration; inherited deadlines keep their original capture. Newly expired rows remain logically invisible and may be swept only under the reactivated schema. Test deletion/reuse, identical replacement, changed audience, nested references and expiry. This adds no device incarnation format.

Restoration, candidate validation, derived rebuild, catalog/generation/programs and current release commit together. Any error rolls all of them back. Retained values overwrite no explicit candidate writes: restoration precedes activation builders. Incompatible name reuse must pass candidate types and constraints; no flag bypasses validation.

## 3. Older writes and incompatible values

Updating an active row while its newer columns are dormant preserves those dormant values. Deleting that row deletes its dormant column values in the same transaction: forward never resurrects a deleted row, including when its id is reused. New rows written under the older schema have no dormant value. On forward they receive the candidate literal default or optional null; required columns without such a default refuse with exact missing-row counts and up to five ids. Repair by deploying a bridge schema with that column optional or a literal default using --restore-dormant, fill it through a bridge mutation, then deploy the desired required schema. Alternatively explicitly delete the named rows through the current release and retry. Never tell the older schema to write a field it lacks. Contextual now/viewer defaults do not invent historical values. Existing required-column backfill rules still apply.

Release activations retain incompatible existing values per row rather than refusing a type narrowing. Section 9 specifies the visibility, reference and restoration rules. Development adoption keeps its checked narrowing behavior. Missing newly required fields still need the bridge described above; retention applies to present historical values, not absent backfill. No implicit coercion or fallback is introduced.

For every data-dependent refusal, aggregate count and bounded samples (five distinct values/keys, 256 UTF-8 bytes each, plus truncation markers) at the failed column/index/constraint, without unbounded row retention. Include actual conflicting values for column failures, index name and conflicting keys for unique failures, and actionable command or schema repair. Detailed values are privileged operator diagnostics only, never ordinary application refusal payloads, query meter diagnostics or sync refusals. No claim that a green read-only preview freezes later writes: adoption rechecks under its transaction.

## 4. Explicit pruning and audit

snapback4 prune <dir> runs through the serving owner when live, otherwise the offline owner. It lists dormant tables, columns and indexes with row counts, logical serialized dormant bytes (including retained asset bytes separately) and at most five ids; it changes nothing without --allow-destructive. With that flag it permanently deletes exactly the currently dormant storage in one writer transaction, records a durable prune event, and leaves active rows/programs untouched. It releases dormant asset homes for the ordinary collector. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive do not prune; direct users to the separate command. Prune intentionally deletes all dormant items as one reviewed storage boundary; per-item dependency-aware pruning is deferred because partial pruning can strand dormant references and asset homes. There is no implicit size or age collector: previews make storage growth visible without inventing a data-loss policy. After prune, reintroducing the schema gets empty tables and normal required-column/default behavior. Pruning is an irreversible operator action; the command output names every item dropped.

Release activation events live in the database transaction with activation revision, operation, release/previous/generation, timestamp and retained/restored summary. Prune records its item summary in that transaction. Existing releases.jsonl and current remain checked convenience copies; post-commit filesystem errors explicitly say activation succeeded. History is never edited or rewound. Rollback picks an earlier immutable source, not a historical data snapshot.

Dormant bytes have no automatic age-based deletion: retention lasts until explicit prune, and prune preview exposes populations so operators control disk use. Dormant asset homes remain live in the asset ledger but are unreadable because the active schema has no home. Active-row deletion also retires any associated dormant asset home. Dormant rows do not run old expiry or jobs while dormant; candidate liveness applies again upon reactivation. Explicit application deletion and ordinary active expiry still mean deletion, not an undo archive.

## 5. Hot activation and ownership

serve owns the database sidecar for its lifetime and publishes a Unix-domain release-control socket in the private per-user runtime directory (§5.1; directory 0700, socket 0600). Check peer credentials on both ends (Linux SO_PEERCRED, Darwin getpeereid), requiring the effective UID. Only the host filesystem owner has this capability; it is not an HTTP door, browser origin, bearer service seat or remote endpoint. Check filesystem objects before replacing a stale socket; never follow symlinks or replace a regular file. Use `.release-command` (a hardened private regular file, no-follow, single link) for the command flock and the deterministic runtime address (§5.1) for the control socket. lstat socket entries; only a same-owner socket may be removed while holding the exclusive store sidecar. Bound command JSON to 4096 bytes and response JSON to 64 KiB, with a 5-second input timeout and 120-second command response timeout. The trusted same-UID operator can directly edit source/database; malicious same-UID edits are outside the threat model, but ordinary source races are caught by a candidate content digest over source/native/worker files in the request and verified by the server. Numeric release id is the only source selector; never a caller path. Commands hold a separate private command flock while allocating/staging immutable release source. They acquire the store owner when stopped; E_STORE_BUSY with a running serve uses the local socket instead of opening another writer. Dev and an older/unreachable server continue to refuse with an exact stop/retry command.

snapback4 releases <dir> reads authoritative current release, activation revision, generation and durable events without adopting source, including while serve is live. This is the recovery command for an ambiguous result. A bounded command message identifies the owned database inode/canonical identity, candidate release id, expected current release and monotone activation revision, operation and explicit flags. The server compiles only its own immutable release directory, prepares native module/worker prerequisites before commit, then holds its existing engine writer guard for activation and in-memory publication. The server verifies both expected current release and activation revision inside the activation transaction. Increment activation revision on every successful release activation, including an equal-hash source change; A→B→A cannot satisfy an old command. Concurrent commands serialize on the command flock; out-of-band stale commands fail compare-and-swap. No request may choose arbitrary filesystem source. Compilation/validation failure leaves the previous engine, bound schema, native module, worker revision and reader factory unchanged.

A query already running in a pinned SQLite read transaction finishes on its old snapshot and reports that generation. A writer already executing finishes before activation obtains its engine guard. Queued HTTP operations are admitted under one release request guard covering signature lookup, argument decoding and execution. Activation acquires its exclusive side before taking the engine guard; admitted operations finish before activation, and later operations dispatch under the new generation. Internal detached readers still fence obsolete identities with E_GENERATION. Fresh requests after publication use the new engine; a stale pooled reader not yet pinned retries through the replacement factory. Prepare a runtime bundle containing bound programs, precompiled native module/metadata, worker root and validated named declarations; prepare the reader factory from the post-activation engine while its writer guard blocks new writers (reader opening failure after commit clears the old factory and uses existing lazy retry, never aborts a committed activation or advertises the old backend). Swap bound/generation/native under the common bound guard in the existing /schema guard order, retire pooled readers, rebind ephemeral state, invalidate change answers, wake sync/polls and update worker revision while holding the writer guard. The supervisor explicitly switches its immutable release project root and named declarations on revision, including addition/removal/replacement; externally supplied --worker stays an operator-owned entry. Worker preparation captures an immutable (root, entry, declarations/environment, supervisor revision) tuple. Under the engine guard it compares that captured revision to the current worker revision before minting credentials; stale preparation is discarded and rediscovered. Never relabel an old prepared source with the latest revision. Credentials bind that checked revision so later activation fences them even when spawn finishes later. Workers restart using the candidate packaged source and native continuation hashes keep the existing conservative parking policy. Long validation can pause writes; hot means no process restart, not an unbounded zero-pause latency promise.

The server continues an admitted activation even if the client hits its 120-second response timeout or closes the socket; failure to write the reply is not permission to roll back or exit the server. The database is authoritative at crash or lost socket reply. Timeout/EOF after sending a command reports an ambiguous result and instructs inspection of current and durable history; never silently resend as a new release or claim failure left the old release active. Before activation fsync required immutable source/native/worker files and their directories, then the published releases directory entry. The supported guarantee covers process crashes and filesystem/SQLite durability on hosts honoring fsync; no guarantee covers failing storage hardware. Restart attaches the database-selected release even if current's convenience copy is stale. After database commit, publication is infallible in-memory assignments and committed ephemeral rebind; fallible native compilation, declaration/environment/import validation happens before commit. Worker process spawn remains fallible after commit: failure reports committed activation with degraded worker availability and retries; it cannot undo a release or external effects. Filesystem-copy failures report the committed result.

### 5.1 Path-independent control address

The accept loop polls a nonblocking listener, but immediately resets each
accepted stream to blocking mode before peer validation or input. Darwin/BSD
inherits the listener's nonblocking flag; Linux does not. Blocking streams
let the existing total input deadline govern clients that write after accept.
If resetting the stream fails, close it and report the accept error; never
admit a command on a stream with unknown I/O mode.

Amendment commissioned and implemented by Codex for Charlie on 2026-10-12,
for the 0.4.24 macOS publication blocker. Darwin's Unix socket path limit
is 104 bytes; the previous deployment-local name failed under ordinary
`/var/folders/.../T/` fixtures. Linux's descriptor-address workaround did not
solve Darwin or provide one portable transport.

Use `/tmp/snapback4-<effective-uid>/<sha256>.sock` on both hosts. The hash is
SHA-256 over the raw bytes of the canonical **deployment directory**, binding
the socket to immutable release source. Aliases that canonicalize to the same path resolve
to one address; two deployments pointing to one external database retain
separate addresses, so only the serving deployment can submit its releases.
At deploy and serve startup, create a missing deployment privately, resolve
its canonical directory once, then use that directory for ownership and all
release paths. Serve also resolves the selected release root before constructing
the project. Worker cwd and `SNAPBACK_PROJECT_DIR` therefore report the same
canonical active release path, including Darwin's `/var` → `/private/var` and
explicit deployment symlinks. An existing deployment alias resolves before
private-directory validation; no symlink is installed inside private storage.
Use the same canonical path spelling on case/normalization-insensitive hosts;
byte-distinct canonical spellings produce distinct addresses. The request
envelope still binds the canonical database path, device and inode.
The longest address is 95 bytes (ten decimal UID digits), below both limits.
Do not use TMPDIR or XDG_RUNTIME_DIR: these may be long or differ between CLI
invocations. This deterministic address needs no durable locator file; deploy
and rollback derive exactly the same address from durable deployment location.
A moved deployment must stop and restart serve, as moving its immutable source
already requires. Socket entries are ephemeral, never backup authority.

Create the single runtime directory atomically as 0700. Open with O_DIRECTORY
and O_NOFOLLOW, inspect the opened metadata, and refuse a different UID, a
non-directory/symlink or any mode other than 0700 (including special bits).
Never repair or chmod an existing directory. `/tmp` is the platform's shared
sticky temporary root (Darwin may alias it to `/private/tmp`). An untrusted
user may pre-create a conflicting name and cause refusal, but gains no access
and causes no modification of their entry. Malicious same-UID filesystem edits
remain outside §5's threat model.

Bind only while holding the exclusive database sidecar. An existing entry
must be a same-owner 0600 socket. Probe it: a live listener refuses without
unlinking; only connection-refused/not-found permits stale replacement. Any
other error refuses. Preserve the inode check around client connect, same-UID
peer checks on both ends, envelope identity, command flock and revision CAS.
Establish inode-based cleanup custody immediately after bind, before chmod or
nonblocking setup; Drop removes only that owned socket inode. Graceful shutdown
joins control before releasing store ownership. Fatal exit after permission setup leaves a stale
socket for the next exclusive owner. A crash between bind and chmod may leave
a socket with the launching umask's mode; if it is not exactly 0600, recovery
refuses and the operator must remove that stale entry. Do not change the
process-global umask in a multithreaded host to hide this fail-closed window. Keep the shared runtime directory on exit:
removing it would race other deployments, and it contains no durable state.

Acceptance: default-TMPDIR project paths exceeding 108 bytes on Linux and
Darwin start serve, execute hot deploy and rollback without restarting, refuse
a second host, clean up on SIGTERM, and restart after SIGKILL. Unit regressions
cover stale/live socket entries, file/symlink and 0600 socket refusals, directory
permissions/type/UID checks, canonical aliases, independent deployments, and
preserving replaced entries during cleanup. SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP also drain
an incomplete control request before process exit; join the control handle
while Shared still retains the owner. An admitted activation is allowed to
finish. Enforce the five-second input bound as a total monotonic deadline,
updating the remaining socket read timeout before every read; partial input
arriving repeatedly cannot reset the deadline or prolong shutdown. The signal
regression keeps sending partial input until the host closes the connection.
No budget or public door changes.

## 6. Devices and partitions

Every changed execution activation including A→B→A gets a fresh monotone semantic generation, never reuses a release number as a generation. Execution-content identity includes the prepared native module digest as well as schema/programs. A changed native digest advances the existing semantic generation atomically with release activation, even when native declarations are unchanged; removing a module changes the digest too. Equal execution content retains generation unless a release activation changes the final active/dormant population (§9.1), which requires a new replica generation; source-only activations still advance activation revision. The digest is SHA-256 over the exact prepared wasm bytes; the no-module sentinel is the empty string persisted as release_native_digest. activation_hash binds schema/programs and that digest. Comparison is with the current activation, so A→B→A is fresh. Startup attaches the persisted execution identity and verifies the immutable release module matches its recorded digest. This uses the existing device protocol, not a separate native epoch. Rollback and forward use the same generation replacement and full partition reacquisition already used by adoption: /schema advertises the exact active shape, sync rejects old watermarks, devices clear obsolete base/index state and rebase or refuse their queued intents under the existing authority/operation contract. Store id remains unchanged; this is not a store replacement. No dormant rows or fields enter snapshots, partition logs, online answers or device stores. Never combine old-generation pages with a new backend. A late old read remains tagged old and cannot overwrite a newly observed generation. Simulate rollback/forward across a delayed sync page and audience changes; retained data changes server storage only, not device persistence formats.

# LLP 3000.000.000.001: Staging with real users

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 deployment, password identity, development doors, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (STAGE)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-18
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Proof kit](./3000.011.003.000.000-packaged-proof-kit.rfc.md), [Store ownership](./3000.019-what-the-builder-still-wrote.rfc.md#34-p8-residency-you-can-see-and-a-cli-that-does-not-fight-the-dev-server)

## 1. Commission and boundary

Implementer: STAGE, 2026-10-18, commissioned by Charlie's wave-16 bakeoff
fixes #1/#4. Ten Muse probes used dev because password-role provisioning
had no single recipe. This amendment supplies that recipe using the existing
signup and service-seat authority. No system seed door is added to serve.
SEC owns self-promotion lints and security invariants; KIT4 owns unrelated
proof helpers, PROD2 owns production starter adaptation.

`deploy <dir> --staging` classifies a **new deployment store**, under its
exclusive owner, with durable metadata `deployment_environment=staging`.
An existing store with no classification is production, including legacy stores;
it cannot be relabelled. A staging store remains staging through deploy,
rollback, restart, copying and source edits. There is no promote/relabel flag:
production requires a separate fresh deployment without --staging and real
operator provisioning. --check never creates the classification. Repeating
--staging on an already staging store is allowed, including while serve runs;
it verifies the existing classification without changing it. Malicious edits by
the filesystem/database owner are outside the threat model, as for service tokens.
This is a deployment-lifetime boundary stronger than a mutable per-release flag:
production source or a seed plan cannot opt a production store into test setup.

## 2. Real staging accounts

`GET /auth/config` reports `development` independently of door availability,
`devDoors`, and `environment` (`development`, `staging`, or `production`).
Missing/unknown environment metadata is production. serve remains bearer-only
in both environments, with the same password verifiers, session lifetimes,
rules, rate limits, clock and data plane. No password hashing or grant bypass.

`startServe(dir, options)` additionally returns `operatorToken(service =
"operator", days = 1)` and `seedAccounts(options)`. Minting uses the existing
local CLI proof protocol, captures secret stdout in memory and never writes
tokens into proof logs. It confers only that service seat's existing authority.
Tokens may also be requested on production for first-admin setup.

`seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` creates N real password accounts per
role, then calls the declared mutation as service:operator; `args(account)`
maps the account to application arguments (default `{principal, role}`).
`provision` is a generated mutation handle or `{name}`, including qualified names such as `accounts.provision`. The returned records
contain email, password, principal, token and expiresAt, grouped by role.
`startServe(dir, {seedUsers: options})` performs the same setup before returning
and exposes those records as `sessions`. Before minting or creating accounts,
the helper requires environment=staging and development=false. Production,
legacy and dev hosts refuse. Inputs are validated before effects; signup is
sequential, bounded, respects identity throttling, and never retries an ambiguous
write. A fresh call creates a fresh random email/password set. Partial failure
does not claim rollback: some accounts/grants may exist; discard the disposable
staging deployment or inspect it before retrying. Application data is created
through ordinary authenticated mutations after provisioning.

Credential records are returned to the operator, never logged by the helper.
The tested guide writes them to an exclusively created 0600 file. They must
not enter source control or reports. There is no public provisioning HTTP
endpoint beyond the application's declared rule-checked mutation.

## 3. First admin

The tested guide uses password signup (which grants no role), then the
filesystem owner's `operatorToken()` to invoke an operator-only mutation.
The roster's insert rule independently requires service:operator. No "first
caller wins", empty-roster bypass, client-selected signup role, or permanent
operator credential in the frontend. The same recipe works on production
with explicit operator-selected email/password; bulk seedAccounts refuses there.
Privileged account tokens cannot mint service tokens. SEC owns lints for
self-bootstrapped global authority; the guide points to this ownership.

## 4. Development doors

For password-only schemas (`auth.password && !auth.guests`), dev disables
`/seed`, every `/dev/*` door (except the existing cryptographically protected
local token protocol), and persona headers by default. The pre-existing serve
GET /dev/latency inspection remains service-authenticated; it never admits an
unauthenticated caller and its reset remains refused. `dev
--unsafe-dev-doors` is explicit opt-in with a prominent warning; serve accepts
no such flag. Host/Origin loopback fences still apply. Door availability is
computed from the current adopted schema, so hot adoption to password-only
also closes them. Seed/reset, clock advance and dev job enqueue revalidate
against the engine schema under the writer immediately before privileged effects;
slow request bodies and requests queued behind adoption cannot retain authority.
Action admission/replay, session-route admission, persona cancellation and upload
owner state transitions likewise revalidate under their actual writer. Previously
admitted durable work retains the existing work-record contract; a refused
new request creates no claimable work.
Persona reads also revalidate against the engine used to evaluate the query,
sync page or asset grant, checking the current declared cast at that read’s
admission. In-flight reads retain their existing snapshot semantics: cast
publication does not lock out concurrent ordinary writes, and the engine
transaction rejects a replaced execution schema.
Change-poll presence entry revalidates under its engine. A detached old
schema is fenced by the engine transaction; a replacement password-only reader
cannot inherit a pre-adoption persona capability. A persona header must name an explicitly declared persona;
an empty declaration admits none. Persona-based schemas retain their ordinary
development workflow. /auth/config always reports development=true on dev,
even with closed doors, so probe tooling can refuse it.

`startDev` follows native persistent storage by default, consuming the old
--no-memory alias; --memory/--fresh explicitly request disposable storage.
The returned now() refuses with guidance if the clock door is closed. Native
readiness plus the per-launch proof marker still establish host identity when
/dev/status is unavailable. Existing fixture/clock tests explicitly opt into
unsafe dev doors when their password schema requires those tools. Generated
frontend proofs explicitly select --memory and --unsafe-dev-doors, preserving
disposable fixture isolation and repeatability under the persistent helper
default. Exact 0.4.30/0.4.31 proof templates are retained and safely adapted
by digest; custom scripts remain untouched. DAY5 owns other starter custody.

## 5. Evidence

Required: production relabelling refusal before writes; staging retention over
hot/cold release switches; real signup/login per role and operator-only grants;
self-grant and unauthenticated doors refused; default/explicit dev doors and
schema-adoption closure; persistent/default and disposable dev; private token
logs; the exact guide recipe executed from the packed tarball in Linux
qualification, including production bulk-seed refusal and first admin; three

exec
/bin/bash -lc 'timeout 10s cat llp/3000.000.000.002-declarative-backfills.rfc.md' in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-mig
