# LLP 3000.000.000.002: Declarative release backfills

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Review
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language, storage, release adoption, staging clock, proof kit
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (MIG)
**Date:** 2026-10-19
**Revised:** 2026-10-19
**Related:** [Release activation](./3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md), [Staging](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md)

## 1. Commission

Implementer: MIG, 2026-10-19. Wave 17's fifty changes require constant,
conditional, derived, enum, string-split and point-lookup transformations.
Declare these in Quarry, with no public migration mutation, job or JavaScript.
REL5 owns release selection/restoration and old-client compatibility; SEC3 owns
security checks. No new version, publication or push authority is implied.

## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Continuous carry-forward keeps the
same normalized definition; changing it under the same name refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse, including fields
owned by a historical source lifetime even when the candidate removes that rule.
Restored narrowed rows retain their individual output ownership, captures and
reference types. Columns added by later predecessor schemas contribute their
own types/defaults only when absent from that archived definition. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
   expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-5`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.

Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Computed and retained/native output values must fit candidate scalar types and
text minimum/format constraints before narrowing; invalid generated outputs
refuse the entire adoption with affected-row samples. Patches remain staged until
all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
New declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot. Inactive
cohorts restore their own still-omitted input interpretations, independently of
new declarations in that activation, and catch up changed authored inputs. Declared historical arguments consume retained
cells and source definitions even when the candidate omits those columns;
retained parent-lifetime bindings still apply. A native-image exception requires a birth marker for this exact declaration,
a matching fingerprint of the original non-output inputs, and valid, complete outputs;
a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
transformation left a retained output. Receipt reference inputs use private namespaced bindings in the existing
dormant parent-lifetime ledger, independently of the live input column. A
retargeted/dropped input cannot substitute its current binding for the saved
reference. Every unchanged saved argument consumed during active or inactive
re-entry validates its private lifetime binding before program evaluation.
Bindings use each restored narrowed row's actual archived reference type
(rather than the table-wide predecessor), even when an
argument is declared as generic `id`. Existing private bindings survive
re-entry with unchanged saved inputs even after the visible column becomes
generic `id`; schema type erasure cannot recapture the same parent id. Reconstruction validates any consumed saved binding before restoring
its value. Original absence is recorded separately from the predecessor's interpreted
value. A physically missing field consumes its source default when present,
otherwise null; receipts retain that interpretation explicitly, preventing
later defaults from changing argument or lookup/index evaluation. Expired sources retire when entering or leaving the
last declaration, including restored table images.

When transformed rows become dormant, candidate output reference types supply
parent bindings even when the predecessor had no such column. Existing invalid
bindings are preserved. If a restored row becomes dormant again, only its
explicitly backfilled authoritative cells update the retained image; original
source definition, other cells and authority provenance stay intact. Forward
selection after rollback reconstructs absent added outputs for that same
declaration before evaluating its arguments, rather than feeding it its own
retained result.

Input omission is stored independently from interpreted null/default values,
both for the current physical lifetime and for each receipt. Ordinary writes
compare interpreted old/new inputs; meaningful edits end omission provenance,
while unrelated writes and default materialization do not. Receipts reconstruct
their original interpretation of omitted fields across later default changes.

Rows inserted while a declaration is active receive a private native-birth
marker keyed by its normalized definition and a hash of their input image;
no original input values are kept in that marker. The same declaration preserves
complete native images with unchanged inputs, including absent optional inputs,
constant transforms and whole-table restoration. Changed native inputs, invalid
present inputs, lost native output images or conflicting other-transform history
refuse with a bridge reason. An inactive overlapping receipt is compatible only
when every overlapping current output equals its recorded preimage; an active
or pruned conflicting history still refuses. Same-column output values belong
to the candidate type, rather than the historical input type, for native rows.
A new declaration name does not inherit another
name's birth marker. Prune retains only native fingerprints for surviving
lifetimes, alongside current omission flags; receipt absence flags are forgotten
with historical values. Delete/reset remove every marker for the ended lifetime.

Source retirement uses the ordinary engine expiry/reference-action closure
under the complete assembled predecessor schema, before any patch runs or
removed table is archived. Only surviving post-action rows enter dormant storage. Restored source
indexes and cap occupancy are rebuilt first; writing-reference groups must fit
the source reservation's live bound. Cascade and set-null effects participate
in this same transaction; restrict or retained-child blockers refuse the entire
adoption. Retired source population forces a new generation so clients cannot
keep children removed by a cascade. Authoritative set-null updates corresponding archived cells/narrowed images
to null before clearing old column bindings, while receipt input bindings remain
independent. An expired
narrowed source is exposed only inside this uncommitted lifecycle phase and
cannot reach a backfill evaluation alive.

Development reset still empties every lifetime's data/history/native/omission
markers, then reconstructs code-derived declaration/input registries from its
retained schema before reseeding; the new identity's rows receive new birth
markers. No release HTTP reset door is added.

Evidence decode is preflighted against
the existing job heap ceiling. Text preview lists private preimages separately
from dormant application rows/cells/index entries.

### Per-column transformed views

A receipt records a `retained_outputs` set independently of its declaration's
active flag. When leaving a declaration, candidate-visible transformed outputs
reverse to their preimages. Outputs absent from the candidate remain transformed
in dormant storage; their set membership survives until reversal or re-entry.
Even an inactive declaration reverses those suspended fields when a later plain
schema exposes them. Whole-table suspension keeps the transformed view intact.

Re-entry preserves the saved preimage for each still-transformed output. A
fully reversed output instead captures its actual assembled source value,
including restored dormant cells and interpreted predecessor defaults, before
applying the patch. Input history and rollback preimages are separate: an input
may retain its original omitted/null interpretation while the latest rollback
view has a literal default. Ordinary writes do not erase omission provenance
when they edit a transformed output from that same original cohort.

An inverse restoring genuine absence records a transaction-local source mask.
Later evaluation cannot fill that absence from the suspended result or a default
belonging to the output schema. The mask is dropped on every exit. Earlier
recorded cohorts' staged patches are replayed into later recorded source views;
fresh declarations still share the untouched predecessor snapshot and cannot
read each other's newly staged outputs. Every decode remains heap-admitted.

Reference arguments copy any matching ordinary cell-lifetime bindings before
restoration consumes that ledger, including transformed reference cells absent
from an archived whole-row definition. Unchanged saved inputs retain existing
private bindings; inferred current bindings cannot overwrite their invalidation.
The stable format is `snapback4-release-store-5`, with schema capability
`release-backfills-v2`. Published 0.4.33's store format 3 upgrades on first use.
Unpublished experimental format 4 and the older capability cannot silently read
this evidence layout. Package versions are not changed in this workstream.

## 4. Staging clock

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification. Each helper control
mints a current staging-time operator session rather than caching a credential
across arbitrary time jumps. Ordinary user sessions retain normal expiry.

Hot release activation samples `shared.now()` under its engine writer, including
the persisted disclosure floor, and passes that one time through the core's
explicit `adopt_release_at`. A live preview probes the existing filesystem owner lock independently of
HTTP bind/address hints and acquires a stopped-store lock before taking its
SQLite snapshot, retaining that lock through inspection. A rollback preview
reuses its caller's existing ownership rather than probing its own lock as a
live host.
A live preview samples time through the same-owner,
store-bound existing private status protocol; no session is minted or clock
changed. A missing existing-store ownership sidecar refuses rather than assuming a
stopped host without exclusion; ordinary rollback preview can reestablish and
reuse its canonical ownership. A revision mismatch with the private database snapshot refuses as a
racing activation. Store contents and the clock are point-in-time samples, not
a freeze of later live writes. A stopped store's cold activation/preview uses
wall time, with the existing persisted disclosure floor applied by activation.
Positive and negative staging offsets are both covered by hot/preview tests.

## 5. Evidence

Tests cover all fifty historical shapes, no-op/restart, v1 writes during
rollback, same-column enum reversal, edited-output refusal, missing lookup,
invalid output, uniqueness failure, deletion/id reuse, generation reset and
hot/cold/preview equivalence. The guide's persistent production recipe uses
real users and operator role setup, tests tightened privacy and declares its
invariant, rolls back and forward with business assertions. Its exact fences
run against the fresh packed tarball. Reproduce five copied bakeoff lanes,
record actual line/time deltas. Run required crate/client/differential/Linux,
Mac Studio clock tests, refs, budget and root checks.

## 6. Reviews

Round 1: [Muse](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r1.muse.txt) and
[fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r1.codex.txt) returned NOT READY.
This revision specifies historical eligibility, exact preimages, lookup changes,
phase order, immutable declaration identities, reference/asset restrictions,
format/generation fences and bounded bridge repairs. It retains strict conflict
refusal rather than Muse's suggested silent edited-row preservation: preserving
a different same-column value under old semantics could change business meaning.
The author explicitly permits rollback refusal with a reason.

The [fifty-shape executable inventory](../snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/tests/backfills/wave17.json)
tracks every line of `~/sb4-loop/wave17/changes.txt`. Exact source solutions are
read-only under `~/sb4-loop/wave17/apps/NNN-snapback-SLUG/`; MIG's copied-lane
reproduction and measured results are recorded in its report.


Round 2: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r2.codex.txt) found four
material cases: dormant membership double application, late narrowed-row
restoration, historical lookup cardinality, and wall-time hot activation. These
now have focused regression coverage and the explicit phase/time rules above.
[Muse attempt](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r2.muse.txt) was unavailable with a
provider subscription-quota error; no verdict is inferred. A later fresh round
reviews the corrected candidate independently.

Round 3: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r3.codex.txt) found
identity output admission, dropped historical reference invalidation, source
expiry retirement, pruned-membership re-entry and deleted-lifetime pruning.
Admission now rejects identity; consumed invalid historical references refuse
regardless of candidate visibility. Every backfill source is retired against
its original expiry before evaluation. Pruned membership forbids re-entry when
historical evidence is unavailable, and prune forgets evidence for lifetimes it
deletes. Each case has a focused regression. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 4: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r4.codex.txt) found
cold activation dropping its persisted time floor when removing the last
`not before`, and absent optional outputs qualifying as a native image.
Activation now clamps all phases to the existing durable floor regardless
of candidate boundaries, resampling under the writer; rebuilds use the same
floor. Native-image exceptions require physically present output fields.
Both cases have regressions. Muse again returned subscription quota exhaustion.

Round 5: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r5.codex.txt) found
new declarations skipping restored outputs, dropped historical arguments,
multiple-declaration snapshot reconstruction, and preview preimage counts.
The corrected implementation records original adoption cohorts, reconstructs
their shared source images/indexes, recovers dropped inputs, stages all cohorts
before applying patches, and lists preimages separately. Focused regressions
cover simultaneous and later declarations, lookups, missing optional arguments,
and new-name transforms. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 6: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r6.codex.txt) found
partial-table restoration omitting a carried declaration's historical image,
and preview using HTTP loopback dialability as an ownership test. Cohort
reconstruction now includes every current declaration in the cohort while
only eligible transformations run. Preview probes the existing owner lock,
holds stopped-store exclusion, and otherwise obtains time privately. Regressions
cover carried parents/restored children and both offset signs on explicit
non-loopback binds. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 7: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r7.codex.txt) found
expiry removal while leaving the last backfill, receipt inputs lacking their
own reference binding after a live retarget, and historical absence acquiring
a newer default. Retirement now covers outgoing history. Each receipt captures
its reference inputs into independent private lifetime bindings; reconstruction
validates consumed bindings before exposing values. Historical absence uses an
explicit null interpretation rather than candidate defaults. Focused regressions
cover all three cases. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 8: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r8.codex.txt) found
new reference outputs lacked candidate-typed bindings when a simultaneous
narrowing archived their row. Dormant classification now captures candidate
output references while preserving invalidation. Regression coverage also
retains transformed cells when an already-retained row becomes dormant again,
and extends historical-absence coverage through rollback/forward. Muse remained
quota-unavailable.

Round 9: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r9.codex.txt) found
optional-input native preservation and later-default absence provenance.
Explicit omission markers now survive interpretation and end on meaningful
application edits. Exact-definition native birth/input fingerprints preserve
valid authored outputs without treating another declaration's history as native.
Tests cover optional and constant backfills, partial/whole table return, new
names, invalid present inputs, unrelated writes, and authored edits returning
to the same value as a later default. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 10: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r10.codex.txt) found
retirement bypassing reference actions and reset clearing code-derived native
registries. Retirement now invokes the existing engine closure on a rebuilt
source snapshot and aborts on blockers, with cap-bound validation and generation
reset for affected data. Reset rebuilds schema metadata before reseeding. Tests
exercise active/restored cascade, set-null and restrict, atomic refusal, and
native inserts/reseeds after reset. Muse remained quota-unavailable.

Round 11: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r11.codex.txt) found
removed source children absent from expiry closure, hidden historical input cells
interpreted as null, and stopped preview copying before ownership. The source
closure now precedes archival, historical arguments consume retained cells and
definitions, and stopped snapshotting holds/reuses ownership. Regressions cover
same-release child removal, hidden optional inputs and stopped rollback previews.

Round 12: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r12.codex.txt) found
generic-id arguments erasing reference bindings and physically sparse defaults
being interpreted as null. Receipt bindings now use historical reference types;
arguments resolve retained cells and predecessor literal defaults before candidate
materialization. Receipts preserve that original interpretation across defaults.
Both cases have regressions. Muse remains unavailable after repeated retries.

The operational deploy card retains its 150-line budget; the new independently
packed-tested production script receives a separate bounded 130-line allowance.
The language card remains within its existing 825-line budget; historical-card
word-reduction target still applies to the original content, excluding only
the new independently compiled backfill example (bounded to 100 words). Original
compiled-block preservation remains unchanged.

Round 13: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r13.codex.txt) found
re-entry replacing a saved binding after visible type erasure, and inactive
receipts losing the original interpretation of omitted defaults. Unchanged
saved inputs preserve existing private bindings. Inactive receipts reconstruct
their still-omitted inputs within their original cohort; fresh declarations
retain their separate current-source snapshot. Regressions cover two table
restoration cycles followed by parent deletion/reuse, default changes across
rollback/forward, and mixed old/new declaration cohorts.

Round 14: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r14.codex.txt) found
set-null source expiry leaving a pre-retirement archived cell and missing owner
sidecars permitting preview without exclusion. Set-null updates archived values
before releasing their binding; restoring after parent id reuse observes null.
A missing sidecar now refuses check/deploy preview; rollback's existing canonical
owner establishes exclusion and is reused. Both have regressions; hot preview
clock parity remains covered at both offset signs and bind configurations.

Round 15: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r15.codex.txt) found
inactive generic-id inputs missing private-binding validation, post-transform
retirement testing old deadlines against patched rows, and text output constraints
being deferred to narrowing. Every consumed unchanged saved input now validates
its binding. Backfill source retirement happens only before patches through the
ordinary action closure; historical lifetime/engine fields refuse as outputs.
Generated and preserved outputs validate text minimum/format constraints before
narrowing. Regressions cover inactive parent reuse, all three reference actions,
and atomic minimum/handle/email refusals. Muse's latest retry again returned the
same provider quota block; no verdict is inferred.

Round 16: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r16.codex.txt) found
restored narrowed rows losing historical reference types and lifetime ownership
under a table-wide predecessor that had erased them. Evaluation and receipt
installation now consult each returning row's archived definition. Original
column types/defaults/ownership stay intact; later predecessor additions supply
only previously absent columns. Regressions cover erased reference restoration
followed by parent reuse, archived expiry/opening fields, and later-added sparse
defaults on returning rows. Decoding provenance remains job-heap bounded.

Round 17: [fresh Codex](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-mig-r17.codex.txt) found
restored output cells missing rollback preimages. The correction tracks each
output's transformed/untransformed view, rather than inferring it from the
immediately previous table schema. This also handles partial field hiding,
authored retained edits, plain-schema return, whole-table suspension and mapping
replacement without accidentally adopting a literal default. Input omission
history remains distinct from the actual rollback view. Additional regressions
cover ordinary bindings for newly transformed references and replay of earlier
recorded cohorts after older-release writes. The unpublished storage/capability
fences advance to reject implementations lacking these field-view semantics.

/// Whether a value is (or contains) the reserved system principal.
pub fn contains_system_principal(value: &Value) -> bool {
    match value {
        Value::Id(id) => id == crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL,
        Value::Array(items) => items.iter().any(contains_system_principal),
        Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) => fields.values().any(contains_system_principal),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Missing optional columns read as null; missing required columns with a
/// literal default read that value. Contextual defaults belong to inserts.
pub fn materialize_optional_fields(row: &mut Value, table: &Table) {
    let fields = match row {
        Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) => fields,
        _ => return,
    };
    for (name, kind) in &table.columns {
        if !fields.contains_key(name) {
            if table.maintained.contains_key(name) {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), Value::Int(0));
            } else if kind.is_optional() {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
            } else if let Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(value)) = table.defaults.get(name) {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), value.clone());
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Borrow complete images; materialize old images only when needed.
pub fn row_image<'a>(row: &'a Value, table: &Table) -> std::borrow::Cow<'a, Value> {
    if table.columns.keys().all(|column| row.field(column).is_ok()) { return std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(row); }
    let mut image = row.clone();
    materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, table);
    std::borrow::Cow::Owned(image)
}

#[cfg(test)]

/// The type an argument declares as a column type, for value validation.
pub fn arg_as_column(kind: &ArgType) -> Option<ColumnType> {
    Some(match kind {
        ArgType::Cursor | ArgType::Scalar => return None,
        ArgType::Id => ColumnType::Id,
    pub fn put(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.put_indexed(table_name, table, row, old, "INSERT INTO i(ti, k, id) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)")
    }

    /// A replacement loader has already cleared the rows and created its
    /// temporary index heap. It must install those indexes before any read.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.4 — native replacement sorts complete indexes inside the publication transaction.
    pub fn put_loading_indexes(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.put_indexed(table_name, table, row, None, "INSERT INTO temp._device_index_load(ti, k, id) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)")
    }

    fn put_indexed(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>, insert_index: &str) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("durable transaction cannot write an ephemeral table".into())); }
        let t = self.num(table_name)?;
        let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
        narrowed::check_write(self,table_name,table,row,old)?;
        backfills::observe_write(self,t,id,table,row,old)?;
        let keys = crate::rows::index_write(table_name, table, row, old, id)?;
        self.changes.borrow_mut().record(table_name, table, old, Some(row));
        let data = canonical_json(row);
        let sql = if old.is_some() { "UPDATE r SET data = ?3 WHERE t = ?1 AND id = ?2" } else { "INSERT INTO r(t, id, data) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)" };
        self.tx.prepare_cached(sql)?.execute(params![t, id, data])?;
        let mut insert = self.tx.prepare_cached(insert_index)?;
        let mut entries = 0;
        for crate::rows::IndexWrite { name, before, after } in keys {
            if before == after { continue; }
            let ti = self.num(&format!("{table_name}.{name}"))?;
            for key in before.iter().filter(|key| !after.contains(key)) {
                self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 AND k = ?2")?.execute(params![ti, key])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
            for key in after.iter().filter(|key| !before.contains(key)) {
                insert.execute(params![ti, key, id])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
        }
        entries += self.update_cap_counts(table_name, table, old, Some(row))?;
        Ok(entries)
    }

    pub fn delete(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, old: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("durable transaction cannot write an ephemeral table".into())); }
        let t = self.num(table_name)?;
        self.changes.borrow_mut().record(table_name, table, Some(old), None);
        let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        dormant::delete(self,t,&id,table_name)?;
        self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id = ?2")?.execute(params![t, id])?;
        let mut entries = 0;
        for (index_name, index) in &table.indexes {
            let ti = self.num(&format!("{table_name}.{index_name}"))?;
            for key in stored_index_keys(table, index, old, &id) {
                self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 AND k = ?2")?.execute(params![ti, key])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
        }
        entries += self.update_cap_counts(table_name, table, Some(old), None)?;
        Ok(entries)
    }

    pub fn next_seq(&self) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        Ok(self.tx.prepare_cached("SELECT COALESCE(MAX(seq), 0) + 1 FROM _commits")?.query_row([], |row| row.get::<_, i64>(0))? as u64)
    }

    /// A meta key written with the transaction (a device's watermark lands
    /// with the rows it covers).
    pub fn set_meta(&self, key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _meta(key, value) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value = excluded.value", params![key, value])?;
        Ok(())
    }

    pub(crate) fn meta(&self, key: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, RuntimeError> {
        Ok(self.tx.prepare_cached("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1")?.query_row([key], |row| row.get(0)).optional()?)
//! Business-state regressions for declarative day-two adoption.
use super::*;
use crate::{Context,Engine,WriteState,wire};
use crate::engine::release::ReleaseActivation;
use serde_json::{json,Value as Json};
fn compile(source:&str)->(Schema,Vec<crate::ir::Program>){
    let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    (serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&b.schema).unwrap()).unwrap(),b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&p.program).unwrap()).unwrap()).collect())
}
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let(s,p)=compile(source);Engine::new_release(Store::memory().unwrap(),s,p,1,None).unwrap()}
fn adopt(e:&mut Engine,source:&str,n:u64)->Result<u64,RuntimeError>{
    let(s,p)=compile(source);let previous=e.store().meta("release").unwrap().map(|s|s.parse().unwrap());
    e.adopt_release(s,p,&ReleaseActivation::new(n,previous),false)
}
fn seed(e:&mut Engine,table:&str,row:Json){let row=wire::row_from_json(&e.schema().tables[table],&row).unwrap();e.seed([(table.into(),row)]).unwrap();}
fn row(e:&mut Engine,table:&str,id:&str)->Value{e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap().get(table,id).unwrap().unwrap()}
fn field(e:&mut Engine,table:&str,id:&str,c:&str)->Value{row(e,table,id).field(c).unwrap().clone()}
const BASE:&str="table bookings:\n  weight: int\n  length: int\n  public 'fixture'\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  delete <- allow\n  online only\nmutation edit():\n  update bookings[id = 'a'] {weight:4000}\nmutation remove():\n  delete bookings[id = 'a']\n";
fn ferry()->String{BASE.replace("  weight: int","  vehicleClass: enum('car', 'van', 'commercial')\n  weight: int")+"backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):\n  {vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 5 else 'car'}\nmutation classify():\n  update bookings[id = 'a'] {vehicleClass:'van'}\n"}
fn write(e:&mut Engine,op:&str){let r=e.mutate(op,None,Context::new("alice",Default::default())).unwrap();assert!(matches!(r.state,WriteState::Sent{..}),"{r:?}");}
#[test]
fn conditional_backfill_rollback_older_writes_and_forward_catchup(){
 let mut e=engine(BASE);seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"a","weight":1000,"length":4}));
 seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"b","weight":1000,"length":5}));
 seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"c","weight":4000,"length":4}));
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();
 for(id,class)in[("a","car"),("b","van"),("c","commercial")]{assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings",id,"vehicleClass"),Value::String(class.into()));}
 assert!(e.store().adoption_report.backfills.iter().any(|s|s.contains("transformed 3 rows")));
 let g=e.generation();adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();assert_eq!(g,e.generation());
 write(&mut e,"classify");
 adopt(&mut e,BASE,1).unwrap();assert!(row(&mut e,"bookings","a").field("vehicleClass").is_err());
 seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"d","weight":3600,"length":1}));
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings","a","vehicleClass"),Value::String("van".into()));
 assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings","d","vehicleClass"),Value::String("commercial".into()));
 adopt(&mut e,BASE,1).unwrap();write(&mut e,"edit");
 let before=e.generation();let error=adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("edited")&&error.contains("historical inputs changed"),"{error}");assert_eq!(before,e.generation());
}

 let mut e=engine(BASE);seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"a","weight":1000,"length":4}));
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,BASE,1).unwrap();write(&mut e,"edit");
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings","a","vehicleClass"),Value::String("commercial".into()));
}
const CUE:&str="table cues:\n  status: enum('standby','done')\n  public 'fixture'\n  update <- allow\n  online only\n";
fn cues()->String{CUE.replace("enum('standby','done')","enum('standby','complete','skipped')")+"backfill cues rename(status: enum('standby','done')):\n  {status: 'complete' if status = 'done' else status}\nmutation skip():\n  update cues['a'] {status:'skipped'}\n"}
#[test]
fn enum_rename_reverses_exact_preimage_and_refuses_post_backfill_edits(){

 assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"cues","a","status"),Value::String("complete".into()));
 adopt(&mut e,CUE,1).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"cues","a","status"),Value::String("done".into()));
 adopt(&mut e,&cues(),2).unwrap();write(&mut e,"skip");let g=e.generation();
 let error=adopt(&mut e,CUE,1).unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("changed after backfill"),"{error}");assert_eq!(g,e.generation());
 assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"cues","a","status"),Value::String("skipped".into()));
}

#[test]
fn string_split_and_ascii_digit_classification_are_deterministic(){
 let source="table people:\n  name: text <=100\n  location: text <=100\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let next=source.replace("  name:","  first: text <=100\n  last: text <=100\n  room: bool\n  name:")+"backfill people parts(name: text <=100, location: text <=100):\n  {first: beforeLast(name, ' '), last: afterLast(name, ' '), room: allDigits(location)}\n";
 let mut e=engine(source);for(id,name,location)in[("a","Ada Lovelace","123"),("b","Prince","１２３"),("c","A B C","")]{seed(&mut e,"people",json!({"id":id,"name":name,"location":location}));}
 adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap();
 for(id,first,last,room)in[("a","Ada","Lovelace",true),("b","Prince","",false),("c","A B","C",false)]{assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"people",id,"first"),Value::String(first.into()));assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"people",id,"last"),Value::String(last.into()));assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"people",id,"room"),Value::Bool(room));}
}

#[test]
fn all_fifty_wave17_change_shapes_run_without_migration_programs(){
 let cases:Vec<Json>=serde_json::from_str(include_str!("../../tests/backfills/wave17.json")).unwrap();assert_eq!(cases.len(),50);
 for case in cases {
  let lane=case["lane"].as_u64().unwrap();let inputs=case["inputs"].as_object().unwrap();let outputs=case["outputs"].as_object().unwrap();
  let columns=|fields:&serde_json::Map<String,Json>|fields.iter().map(|(c,t)|format!("  {c}: {}\n",t.as_str().unwrap())).collect::<String>();
  let old=format!("table rows:\n  legacy: bool default true\n{}  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n",columns(inputs));
  let args=inputs.iter().map(|(c,t)|format!("{c}: {}",t.as_str().unwrap())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
  let next=format!("table rows:\n  legacy: bool default true\n{}  public 'fixture'\n  online only\nbackfill rows change{lane}({args}):\n  {}\n",columns(outputs),case["expression"].as_str().unwrap());
  let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),next.clone())]);assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(),"lane {lane} {}: {:?}\n{next}",case["slug"],bound.diagnostics);
  let mut e=engine(&old);
  for (i,r) in case["rows"].as_array().unwrap().iter().enumerate(){let mut input=r["input"].as_object().unwrap().clone();input.insert("id".into(),json!(format!("r{i}")));seed(&mut e,"rows",Json::Object(input));}
  adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap_or_else(|error|panic!("lane {lane}: {error}\n{next}"));
  assert!(e.schema().public_projection().backfills.is_empty(),"historical transforms never enter device input");
  for(i,r)in case["rows"].as_array().unwrap().iter().enumerate(){
   let got=row(&mut e,"rows",&format!("r{i}"));
   for(c,wanted)in r["expect"].as_object().unwrap(){assert_eq!(crate::wire::to_json(got.field(c).unwrap()),*wanted,"lane {lane} row {i} column {c}");}
  }
  adopt(&mut e,&old,1).unwrap_or_else(|error|panic!("lane {lane} rollback: {error}"));
  for(i,r)in case["rows"].as_array().unwrap().iter().enumerate(){for(c,wanted)in r["input"].as_object().unwrap(){assert_eq!(crate::wire::to_json(&field(&mut e,"rows",&format!("r{i}"),c)),*wanted,"lane {lane} restored {c}");}}
  adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap_or_else(|error|panic!("lane {lane} forward: {error}"));
 }
}
#[test]

//! Reads: point lookups, bounded scans with the read rule hoisted where the
//! prefix decides it, opaque cursors, and merges of ordered lanes. Every
//! candidate row is charged to the meter; a query cut by the meter returns
//! what it found and is `capped` (LLP 2000.000 §6).

use super::authorized_scan::Authorization;

/// Row-image bytes one execution may step over as expired, unswept rows.

// Only the read evaluator mints key evidence, from evaluated inputs, validated
// cursors, or authorized result positions. Storage images cannot call a raw
// scalar/key pricing API; row fields use AuthorizedRow::scalar_heap instead.
struct DisclosedKey<'a>(&'a Value);
struct DisclosedKeys<'a>(&'a [Value]);
struct DisclosedBounds<'a>(&'a Bounds);
impl DisclosedKey<'_> {
    fn heap(self) -> u64 {
        match self.0 {
            Value::String(s) | Value::Id(s) => word_heap::text(s),
            Value::Bytes(b) => word_heap::allocation(b.len()),
            Value::Array(a) => DisclosedKeys(a).heap(), _ => 0,
        }
    }
    fn copy_heap(self) -> u64 {
        match self.0 {
            Value::String(s) | Value::Id(s) => s.len() as u64,
            Value::Bytes(b) => b.len() as u64,
            Value::Array(a) => {
                word_heap::slots::<Value>(a.len()) + a.iter().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).copy_heap()).sum::<u64>()
            }
            _ => 0,
        }
    }
}
impl DisclosedKeys<'_> {
    fn heap(self) -> u64 { word_heap::slots::<Value>(self.0.len()) + self.0.iter().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).heap()).sum::<u64>() }
}
impl DisclosedBounds<'_> {
    fn heap(self) -> u64 {
        let b = self.0;
        [&b.after, &b.at_or_after, &b.before].into_iter().flatten().map(|v|DisclosedKeys(v).heap()).sum::<u64>()
            + [&b.gt, &b.gte, &b.lt, &b.lte].into_iter().flatten().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).heap()).sum::<u64>()
            + b.word.as_ref().map_or(0, |s| word_heap::text(s))
    }
}

fn key_verdict_heap(index: &crate::schema::Index, prefix: &[Value], viewer: &str, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
    // The prefix row's collect buffer, map, Arc, viewer and probe term clones.
    let fields = index.components.iter().zip(prefix).filter_map(|(c,v)| c.column().map(|name| (name, v)));
    let row = fields.map(|(name,v)| {
            word_heap::node::<String, Value>() + word_heap::text(name) + DisclosedKey(v).heap()
        }).sum::<u64>();
    word_heap::vec_growth::<(String, Value)>(prefix.len()) + row + word_heap::slots::<usize>(2) + word_heap::text(viewer) + rule_values(rule, prefix, viewer)
}
fn rule_values(rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr, prefix: &[Value], viewer: &str) -> u64 {
    use crate::schema::{RuleExpr::*, RuleTerm};
    let term = |t: &RuleTerm| match t {
        RuleTerm::Viewer => word_heap::text(viewer), RuleTerm::Literal(v) => DisclosedKey(v).heap(),
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } | RuleTerm::Field(_) | RuleTerm::OldField(_) | RuleTerm::NextField(_) => prefix.iter().map(|v|DisclosedKey(v).heap()).sum(),
    };
    match rule {
        Eq(a,b) | Ne(a,b) => term(a) + term(b),
        And(rs) | Or(rs) => rs.iter().map(|r| rule_values(r, prefix, viewer)).sum(), Not(r) => rule_values(r, prefix, viewer),
        Exists { table, key, equalities, .. } | Created { table, key, equalities, .. } => {
            word_heap::text(table)
            + word_heap::vec_growth::<Value>(key.len()) + key.iter().map(term).sum::<u64>()
            + word_heap::vec_growth::<(String, Value)>(equalities.len()) + equalities.iter().map(|(name,v)| word_heap::text(name)+term(v)).sum::<u64>()
        }
        _ => 0,
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#14 — PAGE admits discovery and target-lane metadata before allocation.
/// Temporary `(table, index)` images built while retaining a rule's complete
/// dependency envelope. Count them without allocating so PAGE lanes can admit
/// the buffer before `rules::probe_sites` clones any names into it.
fn rule_probe_sites_heap(rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
    use crate::schema::{RuleExpr::*, RuleTerm};
    fn term(term: &RuleTerm) -> (usize, u64) {
        term.references().fold((0usize, 0u64), |(count, bytes), (table, _)| {
            (count.saturating_add(1), bytes.saturating_add(word_heap::text(table) + word_heap::text("byId")),
                )
        })
    }
    fn walk(rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> (usize, u64) {
        let add = |(ac, ab): (usize, u64), (bc, bb): (usize, u64)| {
            (ac.saturating_add(bc), ab.saturating_add(bb))
        };
        match rule {
            Eq(a, b) | Ne(a, b) | Compare(_, a, b) => add(term(a), term(b)),
            Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).fold(
                    (1, word_heap::text(table) + word_heap::text(index)),
                    |sum, item| add(sum, term(item)),
                ),
            And(rules) | Or(rules) => rules.iter().fold((0, 0), |sum, rule| add(sum, walk(rule))),
            Not(rule) => walk(rule),
            Allow | Deny | Public { .. } => (0, 0),
        }
    }
    let (count, names) = walk(rule);
    word_heap::vec_growth::<(String, String)>(count).saturating_add(names)
}

fn metered_dependency_table(
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — one prepaid, restoring scope: a partial conjunction denial costs the same static allowance as a uniform denial.
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        });
        self.trace_authorization(&verdict, name, Effect::Read, "read rule");
        let verdict = verdict?;
        if !verdict { self.filtered_clause(name, "read rule"); }
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if uniform && admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

    /// A page guard, judged once for the caller (`rules::page_guard_decision`):
    /// a row-independent grant, or a disjunct whose row columns the prefix pins,
    /// evaluated with the prefix's values. It charges the read rule's static
    /// probe count on either verdict, and records no filtered clause: a false
    /// guard leaves the per-row walk.
    fn grant_verdict(&mut self, table: &Table, index: &Index, prefix: &[Value], rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(bool, super::expiry::Pending), RuntimeError> {
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let probe_row = prefix_row(index, prefix);
        let viewer = self.context.viewer.clone();
        let verdict = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(rule), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read))], |runner, allowance| {
            rules::evaluate(rule, &viewer, None, &probe_row, false, &mut PrefixProbe { runner, allowance })
        })?;
        let admitted = verdict && !self.read_capped();
        Ok((admitted, if admitted { expiry.defer() } else { Default::default() },
        ))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — coverage applies before a missing candidate or empty page can return zero/complete.
    pub(super) fn aggregate_read_coverage(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], site: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.aggregate_read_coverage_inner(table, index, key, site, false)
    }

    fn aggregate_read_coverage_inner(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], site: &str, metered: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if metered { self.metered_aggregate_source_dependency(table)?; }
        else { self.aggregate_source_dependency(table); }
        if !self.device { return Ok(()); }
        let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table) else { return Ok(()); };
        if self.defer_receipt_maintain(maintain) {
            let group = self.receipt_read_group(maintain, index, key)?;
            self.receipt_maintain_pending(maintain, group.as_deref())?;
        }
        if let Some(k) = &maintain.row_key {
            // A projected view uses its member's acquisition; absence beyond that
            // horizon is unknown even though the projection has no order columns.
            if self.schema.tables[&k.table].sync.as_ref().is_none_or(|s| s.horizon.is_some()) {
                if metered { metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &k.table)?; }
                else { self.read_tables.insert(k.table.clone()); }
                // A held point value is checked against the member by live_row_view.
                let index_def = &self.schema.tables[table].indexes[index];
                if !index_def.unique || key.len() != index_def.components.len() {
                    let whole = self.schema.tables[&k.table].sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|sync| {
                        let Some(horizon) = &sync.horizon else { return false; };
                        let group = match &sync.audience {
                            crate::schema::Audience::Public => Some(vec![]),
                            crate::schema::Audience::Column(field) => index_def.columns().iter()
                                .position(|f| f == field).and_then(|i| key.get(i)).map(|v| vec![v.clone()]),
                            _ => None,
                        };
                        group.is_some_and(|group| {
                                matches!(self.tx.acquired_boundary(&k.table, &horizon.by, &group),
                            Ok(Some(crate::rows::AcquiredBoundary::Whole)))
                            })
                    });
                    if !whole { self.unknown_fact(table, index, key, site); }
                }
            }
            return Ok(());
        }
        let source = &self.schema.tables[&maintain.source];
        if self.schema.maintain_subset_unknown(table) || (source.is_expiring() && source.sync.as_ref().is_none_or(|sync| sync.horizon.is_some())) {
            if metered { metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &maintain.source)?; }
            else { self.read_tables.insert(maintain.source.clone()); }
            self.unknown_fact(table, index, key, site);
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn horizon_coverage(&mut self, scan: &Scan, prefix: &[Value], retained: bool) {
        if retained { self.beyond_horizon = true; self.components.note(|frame| { frame.beyond = true; frame.retained = true; }); }
        else { self.unknown_fact(&scan.table, &scan.index, prefix, &scan.site); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.016#2.2 — unknown inbox coverage withholds the whole query, including unshaped pages.
        if self.schema.timelines.iter().any(|timeline| scan.table == timeline.table("Feed")) { self.query_unavailable = true; }
    }

    /// Whether a row is past its table's `expire` column at this run's
    /// clock. Expired is absent: to every reader, on every host, before
    /// any rule (the sweep makes it physical). The system sees no more
    /// than anyone: an expired row contributes to nothing.
    pub(super) fn expired(&self, table: &Table, row: &Value) -> bool {
        self.context.now.is_some_and(|now| table.expired(row, now))
    }

    /// Whether a stored row is outside its published half-open read lifetime.
    /// Physical write resolution and cleanup use `expired` instead. A due row

//! Host startup; request scheduling remains in serve.rs.
use super::*;
pub fn serve(
    project: Project,
    port: u16,
    mut mode: Mode,
    bind: &str,
    inherited: Option<std::net::TcpListener>,
    capacity: crate::host_limits::Capacity,
) -> Result<(), String> {
    crate::host_limits::initialize();
    let capacity = capacity.fit()?;
    crate::host_limits::install(capacity);
    let mut source_stamp = project.sources_stamp();
    let bound = project.compile().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        let verbose = !matches!(mode, Mode::Dev { verbose: false, .. });
        let (reports, hidden) = crate::verbs::problem_reports(&bound, true, verbose);
        let shown = reports.len();
        for report in reports {
            snapback4_core::diagnostic!("{report}");
        }
        if hidden > 0 {
            snapback4_core::diagnostic!(
                "snapback4 dev: {hidden} redundant cascade diagnostic(s) hidden; use --verbose to show them"
            );
        }
        return Err(format!(
            "{shown} problem{} in snapback/",
            if shown == 1 { "" } else { "s" }
        ));
    }
    let dev = matches!(mode, Mode::Dev { .. });
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — replay suppresses new effects and holds schedule admission; ordinary holds keep their separate meanings.
    let replay = matches!(mode, Mode::Dev { replay: true, .. });
    let hold_effects = matches!(
        mode,
        Mode::Dev {
            hold_effects: true,
            ..
        }
    );
    let hold_schedules = replay
        || matches!(
            mode,
            Mode::Dev {
                hold_schedules: true,
                ..
            }
        );
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#4-staging-clock — only immutable store classification admits a release test clock.
    let staging = if let Mode::Release { owner, .. } = &mode {
        owner.open_store(Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.meta("deployment_environment").map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() == Some("staging")
    } else { false };
    let clock = crate::dev_clock::Clock::new(
        dev || staging,
        match mode {
            Mode::Dev { clock_offset, .. } | Mode::Release { clock_offset, .. } => clock_offset,
        },
    )
    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let generated = if dev {
        Some(project.write_generated(&bound).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?)
    } else {
        None
    };
    if dev {
        project.check_server_at(&mut source_stamp)?;
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — only the owning host may replace replay/schedule policy; deployment shares the same lock.
    // Release startup already owns this lock before selecting its source.
    let mut host_lock = if matches!(mode, Mode::Dev { fresh: false, .. }) {
        Some(crate::store_owner::host(&project.store_path())?)
    } else {
        None
    };
    // A busy port is a failed start, not permission to change the stopped host's policy.
    let server =
        crate::witness_socket::server(bind, port, inherited, capacity.connections(), || {
            crate::listen_refusal::in_use(bind, port, if dev { "dev" } else { "serve" })
        })?;
    let address = server
        .server_addr()
        .to_ip()
        .ok_or("HTTP listener has no IP address")?;
    if let Some(file) = &mut host_lock {
        crate::store_owner::announce(file, address)?;
    }
    if let Mode::Release { owner, .. } = &mut mode {
        // @ref LLP 3000.019#3.4 — publish through the acquired handle, including a release database symlink.
        crate::store_owner::announce(
            Arc::get_mut(owner).ok_or("release owner already shared")?,
            address,
        )?;
    }
    let host_owner = match &mode {
        Mode::Release { owner, .. } => Some(owner.clone()),
        _ => host_lock.take().map(Arc::new),
    };
    if let Some(owner) = &host_owner { owner.report_reclaim(matches!(mode, Mode::Dev { verbose: true, .. })); }
    // `--fresh` has private, disposable storage and the same WAL readers as dev.
    let fresh = if matches!(mode, Mode::Dev { fresh: true, .. }) {
        Some(query_readers::FreshDirectory::new()?)
    } else {
        None
    };
    let mut engine = match &mode {
        Mode::Dev { fresh: true, .. } => Engine::new_at(
            fresh.as_ref().unwrap().store().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
            bound.schema.clone(),
        run.chunks, if run.chunks == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, run.rows_written, if run.rows_written == 1 { "" } else { "s" });
    respond(request, 200, json!({ "job": run.name, "id": run.id, "state": run.state, "chunks": run.chunks, "chunkSize": run.chunk_size, "retries": run.retries, "heapPeak": run.heap_peak, "rowsWritten": run.rows_written, "error": run.error }));
}

pub(super) fn clock_status(request: Request, shared: &Shared) {
    if let Err(why) = clock_service(&request, shared) { return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})); }
    let result = (|| {
        let (epoch, reader) = shared.reader()?;
        let note = crate::dev_clock_store::ahead(&reader, shared.now());
        lock(&shared.readers).put(&epoch, reader.into_inner());
        note
    })();
    match result {
        Ok(note) => { let now = shared.now(); respond(request, 200, json!({"storeAhead":note,"now":now,"skewMs":now - snapback4_core::now_ms()})) },
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
    }
}

    /// A row by id, materialized, without a rule check.
    pub(crate) fn fetch(&mut self, _allowance: &Authorization, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        let found = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            self.live_lookup(_allowance, table_name, "byId", &table.indexes["byId"], &[Value::Id(id.into())], #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
        } else { self.authorized_scratch(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table), &[], |runner, authority| {
            runner.tx.get(authority, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                        found.map(|mut row| {
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                row
            })
                    })
        })? };
        if self.device && (table.sync.is_none() || (found.is_none() && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|s| s.horizon.is_some()))) { self.unknown_fact(table_name, "byId", &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())], "write target"); }
        match found {
            Some(row) if !self.inactive(table_name, &row)? => Ok(Some(row)),
            _ => Ok(None),
        }
    }

    /// A server write may address an unopened row without making that image
    /// readable. Fixed decode admission prevents its payload size from
    /// selecting a public meter result.
    pub(crate) fn fetch_write_target(
        &mut self,
        allowance: &Authorization,
        table_name: &str,
        id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        if self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .is_none_or(|table| table.not_before.is_none())
        {
            return self.fetch(allowance, table_name, id);
        }
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        if self.device {
            self.unknown_fact(
                table_name,
                "byId",
                &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())],
                "scheduled write target",
            );
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let found = self.authorized_scratch(
            crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table),
            &[],
            |runner, _| {
                runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                    found.map(|mut row| {
                        materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                        row
                    })
                })
            },
        )?;
        Ok(found.filter(|row| !self.expired(table, row)))
    }

    /// The scan's prefix values, evaluated. A word term is not a value a
    /// store can seek to; `scan_words` takes those scans apart first.
    pub(super) fn prefix_values(&mut self, scan: &Scan) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        scan.prefix
            .iter()
            .map(|key| match key {
                IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) => self.expr(expr),
                IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_) => Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a matching term is read through its words(..) index, never as a prefix value".into())),
            })
            .collect()
    }

A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or a declarative `backfill table name(fields: types): {column: expression}`; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
```
`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. Preview runs exact adoption on a private disk snapshot and removes it, without activating; disk/time scales with the store, and later writes can change the plan. Release is immutable source; latest is the highest successfully adopted source even after rollback. A refused candidate is never selectable. Bare rollback selects the previously active release; rollback prints the resolved target before preview or activation. Generation is a monotone identity; releases N/latest inspects source. Identical source is a no-op. Deploy/rollback return after serve switches: `release N is current as generation G`. In-flight one-shot operations finish in their generation; no settle sleep is needed. Default adoption is one measured receipt; --verbose/--json includes phases/cache advice. N_ADOPTION_PAUSE appears above one second. A dev/older unreachable owner requires stopping that owned process before retrying; failed adoption keeps current.

Widened enum values, or short codes a loose release wrote, become dormant rows when selected types cannot hold them; compatible forward restores exact values. Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Point reads/updates/ deletes see absent rows; dormant ids stay reserved. Nominal reference values stay legal, as for other missing ids; require/read-through decides live-target admission. Dormant rows occupy no cap/unique slots, so older writes can require a bridge repair. Removed tables/columns/indexes also stay dormant. Fresh-source name reuse needs --restore-dormant; rollback selects restoration explicitly. Old active updates keep dormant cells; active deletion removes them. Only active referential actions run: parent deletion invalidates dormant lifetimes. Narrowed rows with invalid visible references stay dormant; whole-table/column restoration retains its E_ADOPT barrier. Prune previews counts/bytes/sample ids; `prune ./deployment --allow-destructive` permanently drops all dormant storage. No age collector or implicit pruning exists. Deploy/rollback --allow-destructive bypasses neither validation nor retention. --allow-feed-adoption permits the explicit feed launch. Inspect releases after an ambiguous reply. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## Bridge a safety gate before rollback
Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.

1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.

## What the old release sees
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
```js
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1'); const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
assert.equal(absent.status, 404); assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest'); assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');
```
Previews lead with removed guards and their refusal codes, then group query/count active-table losses, linked dormant rows and safety dependencies per change. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Original read-through captures restore only with the same capture definition; new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.

## Clients across rollback
Save versions/v1/schema.q (and any other .q modules with their original names) outside snapback/ and create `snapback/client-compatibility.json` containing `{"baseline":"versions/v1","since_release":2}`. `check`/`dev` emit added row/result fields and existing-operation parameters as optional, labelled "Since release 2", retaining older result value domains. This is your release label, not server generation; ordinary types stay required without opt-in. Use `row.color ?? "none"` for the baseline fallback. Keep contemporary fixtures in versions/v1/fixtures/; deploy with `--schema versions/v1` and test with `--source versions/v1` (guide testing). Baselines resolve from the original project root, including saved-source deploys.

`useRelease()` from snapback4/react follows the authenticated catalog even when query gates are closed: loading, denied, or `{generation, operations, tables}` with public argument/field shapes. Generated `api.op.available(release)` is false while unready or when name/kind/argument signatures differ. This checks capability, not authorization or unchanged business semantics; still handle E_OP/E_INPUT and authored guard refusals when a switch races it. The SDK withdraws old metadata on observed generations, polls while subscribed, and returns loading offline/on catalog failure; account retirement/close withdraws it. Keep draft state above generation-sensitive read/action gates; remount only the gated children. Dormant means unavailable on this release, not deleted. Test v2 → v1 → v2 with one frontend: field fallbacks, missing operations, retained drafts and restored values. Alternatively check selected immutable source in a separate copy and rebuild its matching frontend. Preserve deployment source; Expo/Exact runtime changes also require a device rebuild.

## Trigger a deployed job
A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.
